commission (de) mr president, mr varela suanzes-carpegna, i too welcome your report, because i see that we agree on the progress made in the protection of fisheries resources and ecosystems in the north sea. i also think that the commission's successes in monitoring measures speak for themselves. i can assure you that in the future i will follow the method adopted. i would also like to point out that following the bilateral negotiations with norway at the end of last year, it was agreed to establish quota measures for cod, haddock, plaice, plaice and mackerel. i would also like to point out that similar measures have already been introduced in the past, in collaboration with norway, for north sea herring. these medium-term measures are in line with the precautionary approach to exploiting fisheries resources recently proposed by the international council for the exploration of the sea. the commission intends to extend measures in the medium term to species outside the north sea. this is already the case for some important species in the baltic sea, such as herring and sprat. i am assuming that the application, as from 1 january this year, of the new regulation on the conservation of fishery resources through technical measures for the protection of juveniles of marine organisms will considerably improve the protection of juvenile fish, not only in the north sea but also in all community waters of the north-east atlantic. in addition to the minimum landing sizes already in place, we have provided in this new regulation for minimum sizes for mussels and crustaceans. we introduced a ban on spear fishing off the north-east coast of england and the east coast of scotland and provided that trawlers could only fish for shrimp with separating trawls or with nets fitted with a sorting grid. in addition, the provisions of the old regulation on technical measures have of course been taken over and simplified. here again, i will give you a few examples: we have grouped the mesh sizes so that a given mesh size is valid for small, medium and large species. thus, a uniform size of 100 mm applies to all large species in northern waters. in southern waters, a dimension of 77 mm is valid for hake and other species. in order to allow the juveniles to escape, lobster and crab fishing in northern waters must use square mesh nets with mesh that cannot close. as you know, the catching of lobsters often involves the taking of large quantities of juveniles of other species. these are just a few examples. as you can see, these measures should ensure greater selectivity. of course, we will continue to monitor the situation closely after these new measures are introduced. i hope this development is good.
the rapporteur has already addressed the problems of resource management and environmental protection on several occasions. this commission communication is part of the commission's overall strategy to improve the integration of environmental considerations into other community policies. the commission's overall strategy is to ensure that fishing activities reach economically and environmentally sustainable levels through the management of scarce fish stocks, in order to avoid their depletion due to overexploitation and/or environmental degradation. without effective management, the fisheries industry will experience economic decline as stocks decline and demand for seafood becomes increasingly dependent on imports. i believe that the spanish press has called me an enemy of the fishing industry for having said that. it goes without saying that i am as much an enemy of the fishing industry as those who advise people not to saw the branch of the tree on which they are sitting. that is not wise. in particular, the communication calls for the reduction of fishing pressure in order to preserve commercial stocks and the marine ecosystem, improved measures for the conservation of the marine environment in integrated coastal zone management, improved training, transparency of information and an increased contribution of scientific research to fisheries management. all the priorities mentioned above will be pursued both at community level and in the various international forums in which the community participates. they should also be pursued in the framework of bilateral and multilateral agreements negotiated by the community. while the communication reveals the commission's good intentions in this area, it also reveals what activities are missing and where management and control improvements can be made. we can quote passages from the communication. over-fishing is such a problem in the community fisheries that the scientific authorities have recommended a rapid reduction in fishing levels by 40%. this was stated in the communication published last july. since then, the fisheries council, meeting on 16 december, has decided to impose drastic reductions in the total allowable catches of certain species in the region, such as cod and anchovy, of 25% for cod and 43% for anchovy for the year 2000. in addition, the commission has adopted emergency regulations for a recovery plan for cod in the irish sea, which actually calls for a total cessation of cod fishing in the irish sea between 14 february and 13 april because of a crisis in the species. these are just some of the acute problems facing the fishing industry because of overfishing. what can be done to mitigate the damage to fish farming communities? firstly, it should be recognised that community legislation needs to be strengthened and that the control mechanism procedures need to be improved. secondly, the principles of such community legislation should also be extended to international agreements to which the community is a party. thirdly, the european union should commit itself to improving the monitoring and prevention of the significant pollution of community waters which is degrading marine fauna and flora, including fisheries resources. more controls should be put in place to prevent accidental oil spills like the erika in the bay of biscay, as well as deliberate spills of waste oils. the pressures that urbanisation of coastal areas places on the fragile marine ecosystem should also be recognised. the pressures are most acute in europe. other problems include the discarding of unintentionally caught species, the destruction of the seabed by fishing gear and the destruction of natural habitats by over-intensive aquaculture. an alternative could be the introduction of a quota management system such as that which has existed in new zealand since 1986. through this system and taking into account the total allowable catches for the species, a market is created for individual quotas of fishing rights in a certain area which the holders can trade with each other, exploit themselves or rent to other fishermen for a certain period of time. under this system, the right to own property is the right to fish - not the fish. such a market organization is likely to create better long-term prospects for the fishing industry. today, ladies and gentlemen, we can never be fully informed about the vast ecosystem of the sea or the many factors that influence the stocks of marine aquatic resources. however, we should not ignore scientific research, we should not stop gathering information, we should not stop trying to improve existing systems and, if necessary, we should at least consider changing our policies. the fisheries sector is in enormous difficulty in europe and i am a great supporter of the fisheries sector. i therefore call on the commission and the council to find the information and political will to provide long-term solutions for the fishing communities and not to limit themselves to the short term. i recommend this report to parliament.

- (el) i would like to say that a charter of fundamental rights is welcome and necessary, provided that we avoid the three dangers that lie in wait for us. the first danger is that it will be turned into a public relations treaty, of which the european union has given us many examples, such as the one on employment, which is devoid of legal substance, is binding and does not offer the citizen the possibility of claiming the exercise of his rights before the courts. the second danger is that the european union will use the charter as a pretext to continue to ignore the fundamental decisions and provisions of international bodies, such as the european convention on human rights or the various decisions of the international labour organisation. and the third danger is that we will end up with a general charter, a medium-term instrument that is not very rigorous, a mechanism which, in the end, would call into question the advanced human rights achieved in various countries. it could be imagined, for example, that a country grants the right to free education, and that in a charter of the european union, the general reference to the right to education is a first step towards calling this right into question. if these three pitfalls are avoided, if the charter deals seriously with social rights, if it is open to the issues raised by the internet, new technologies, etc., and if it applies to everyone living in europe, then it can make a positive contribution.

commission. - (de) the agreement signed between the commission and the bulgarian government on 29 november 1999 is in line with the commission's general policy. this policy consists in shutting down as early as possible reactors which cannot be upgraded at reasonable costs to internationally acceptable safety standards. the agreement between the bulgarian government and the commission provides for the final shutdown of units 1 and 2 of the kozloduy nuclear power plant before 2003. it is also expected that the bulgarian government will take a decision in 2002, in agreement with the commission, on the timetable for decommissioning units 3 and 4 of the same plant. all the blocks will be stopped before the dates originally planned by the bulgarian government. originally, it was planned to close blocks 1 and 2 of kozloduy in 2004 and 2005 and blocks 3 and 4 in 2008 until 2010. the commission assumes that the final shutdown of reactors 3 and 4 will take place by 2006 at the latest. in order to help bulgaria overcome the problems that will arise from the closure, the commission is providing substantial multiannual assistance to bulgaria. this includes grants of eur 200 million under the phare programme and a euratom loan. the elements of the abovementioned agreement form an indivisible whole. in order to ensure full implementation of the agreement, the second half of the grants will be confirmed only after final agreement on the closure dates of blocks 3 and 4. the two components of the aid package - both the euratom loan and the grants - will depend on whether and how bulgaria fulfils its commitment to shut down the reactors. on 4 february 2000, the joint commission-bulgaria working group on the kozloduy power plant met in sofia. the working group agreed to draw up a work plan on the closure of units 1 and 2 by may or june 2000 and to present a policy framework and potential projects in the energy sector to be financed through the aid package. the commission notes that the decision to close the airport was not an easy one for bulgaria. the decision shows, however, that in the context of the enlargement process the bulgarian government is complying with the obligations laid down by the european union to improve safety standards in the nuclear sector. the commission will continue to cooperate with the bulgarian government in order to implement the agreement of last november. this cooperation is also part of the accession partnership which gives priority to this mission.

- (el) mr president, ladies and gentlemen, it is commendable that efforts are being made to set limit values for pollutants resulting from the incineration of waste and to put in place precise control procedures. in fact, it is intolerable for pollution to be transferred from the atmosphere to water and soil. the directive marks important progress in this respect. the problems inherent in incineration remain, however, as the process itself generates new toxic substances, including dioxins. this is especially true of household waste, which, before being incinerated, contains negligible amounts of toxic substances. thus, applying the same treatment to hazardous and non-hazardous waste only aggravates the problem. in addition, the cost of developing and operating incineration plants remains much higher than that of mechanical recycling units and landfilling. in summary, the legislative proposal under consideration does not solve the problems inherent in incineration as a waste management method.
- (el) mr president, the first report i drew up as a member of parliament dealt with exactly the same subject as the one we are discussing today: the environment and the structural funds. and i came, of course, to the essential conclusion that actions financed by the structural funds should in no way harm the environment of the member states and that, to this end, greater importance should be given to the environmental dimension at all levels of programming and implementation of the relevant union policies. this is what i am saying again today, that our main objective must always be sustainable development - development that respects and promotes the environment. i therefore believe that in this direction the european parliament, in its resolution, should call on the commission to implement the structural funds programmes in a way which takes account of community environmental legislation, particularly as regards the directives on the conservation of natural habitats and wild birds. but at the same time we must avoid the excesses of stopping all programmes and not approving any if they do not guarantee compliance with one or two regulations. it reminds me of a greek proverb that says you can't cut off your head for a toothache. i'm not suggesting that we break the law. i propose instead that the programmes be implemented while monitoring the application of the legislation and naturally ensuring that parliament is informed by the commission. it is important to remember that the structural funds support major measures and actions which are aimed primarily at the most problematic countries and regions of the union, which have great needs in their hard work to develop and catch up with the more developed member states. and we must also remember, mr president, that the structural funds often make it possible to promote measures to protect the environment. if we stop all programmes without exception for the reasons that many colleagues in this house have given, we will ultimately do more harm than good to the environment.
- (el) mr president, i believe that ensuring the full and correct implementation of community environmental policy is perhaps the greatest challenge facing the member states today. but unfortunately, environmental policy requirements are not being respected and are not being integrated, as they should be, into the development and approval of programmes and projects in the member states. the problem is further aggravated by the fact that, apart from violations of environmental legislation for works and programmes carried out with community funds and approval, fraud also occurs in the course of the implementation of works and programmes which are the subject of private and national funding. i would draw your attention to paragraph (b) and paragraphs 3 and 8 of the resolution, which expressly mention this issue. it is inconceivable that the application and compliance by the member states with the european union's environmental legislation should not cover all projects, whether or not they require community or other funding. therefore, countries which violate environmental legislation must be well aware that the commission, as the guardian of community primary and secondary law, will ensure that the environment is not degraded by the construction of works. the continued flagrant violation of key environmental legislation is unacceptable. and finally, we must say what is happening in the member states and why a correct policy requires transparency, courage and truth. and the truth is that the implementation of the natura 2000 directives is failing, in my country as in the other member states. for example, if the maliac gulf and the spercheios river water biotope have been proposed as protected areas of the natura 2000 network, it is planned to link the two shores of the maliac gulf by a bridge, in violation of community rules, when this could be done by using another solution. i therefore urge that we urge the commission to ensure that community environmental legislation is applied effectively and respected for all projects and, if necessary, to refer matters to the european court of justice, as a colleague has said, to recommend large fines and to demonstrate rigour.
- (el) speaking today on the occasion of the 50th anniversary of the geneva convention for the protection of human rights during and after conflict, i am very sorry to find myself in the difficult position of saying that this convention has been flouted within the european continent itself. all the events in the balkans in recent years, when, in the words of a great greek poet, 'without discretion, without shame' an attempt was made to make good an evil by a multitude of similar evils, have simply turned this convention into a fine literary text without practical significance. we have recently experienced a terrible humanitarian disaster in an intervention purportedly for humanitarian purposes. we have seen a huge influx of refugees and at the same time we have seen bombings of hospitals and embassies, civilians being targeted, and for many years to come the balkans will pay the price and suffer the painful consequences of the ecological disaster caused by the bombings. as regards the second chapter of the convention, in particular articles 13 and 14 which deal with the protection of the wounded, sick, elderly, children and pregnant women against the consequences of war, it is really worthwhile to dwell on the case of kosovo and on all that the un report reveals on this subject. nearly half of all premature babies die. all newborns are underdeveloped due to malnutrition. abortions are skyrocketing. many women die from lack of basic sanitation in clinics that have no electricity, heating or water. the best way to commemorate the 50th anniversary of the geneva convention is therefore to strengthen the democratic and political character of the union, so that it can play a more decisive role on the international scene in resolving disputes of any kind, by peaceful means of respect for human rights and respect, above all, for all that is set out in this truly admirable geneva convention.
- (el) mr president, we are particularly concerned about the hypocritical and selective exploitation of human rights in the exercise of the union's foreign policy and, above all, as a pretext for interventions that even take on a military character. at the same time, the european union is turning a blind eye to cases of brutal violations of human rights, naturally promoting its imperialist interests. a striking example is his attitude towards the turkish regime. the recent invasion of kosovo, which is continuing under the guise of defending the rights of minorities, and the incredible demands being made on yugoslavia are a vivid example of how military intervention is being constructed as a general principle of international law to the detriment of the principle of national sovereignty. moreover, the policy pursued by the union within its borders violates in practice a whole series of fundamental human rights. political and social rights are being trampled underfoot, individual rights and democratic freedoms are being trampled underfoot. the results of this policy are already being felt with the rise of fascist and racist forces, and not just in austria. however, the report on the fight against racism does not indicate that the necessary conclusions have been drawn. we call on the peoples to intensify their struggle against the questioning by those who proclaim themselves their saviours of the rights they have won with hard fighting and sacrifices, and to reverse the current course of things which serves only their exploiters.
- (el) mr president, it is undeniable that in recent years, along with the increase in immigration, a climate of prejudice has begun to take hold and cases of racial violence have multiplied, while political parties have been created on the basis of a racist ideology. in other words, a social climate is being created, as was shown this morning in some parts of our parliament, which shows that the protection of human dignity, respect for differences, democratic values and social solidarity have not yet been fully achieved. this is why our action to respect human rights and to build a just society in which human beings coexist creatively must be part of a wide range of policies and measures to improve economic and social conditions for all, to combat unemployment, poverty and social exclusion. the new multicultural reality of europe must be accompanied by policies that ensure the integration of immigrants and the various groups and minorities into the social, political and economic life of national societies. we certainly need a common european policy on asylum and immigration, based on european humanist values. of course, the field of education is also important and great improvements need to be made in the education of the children of immigrants and roma. at the same time, the media are called upon to contribute to the fight against prejudice, by highlighting the beauty of the diversity and multiculturalism of contemporary societies. naturally, in this context, respect for all those who invoke the conscience clause, for example when they are recruited, must be taken for granted. i would like to take this opportunity to assure the european parliament that the problem that once existed in greece no longer exists, following the adoption and entry into force of the relevant legislation on 1 january 1998. the reference to this in mr haarder's otherwise remarkable report is therefore now irrelevant.
in writing. - (el) another european summit, this time at the initiative of the portuguese presidency, will be held to promote proposals and measures to support employment and the fight against unemployment, and to make its own contribution to the intensification and strengthening of the neoliberal and monetarist policies which are responsible for the current unfavourable situation, which are opposed to any social and employment policy and lead to a dramatic increase in unemployment, job insecurity, discrimination, social exclusion and the generalization of poverty. it is a contradiction, an irony, a hypocrisy and a provocation against workers to propose as measures to combat unemployment the strengthening of the competitiveness of enterprises by lowering the cost of labour, the complete liberalization and flexibility of the labour market, the extension of atypical forms of employment. the maintenance and strengthening of the stability pact, the priority given to nominal convergence criteria, and the full subordination of social policy to financial requirements and criteria are precisely what we should strike and eliminate first if we want to talk about genuine employment policies. taxation of speculative capital movements, the abolition of tax havens, the fight against fraud and tax evasion, the redistribution of wealth and income in favour of labour, control of aid to enterprises and the immediate cessation of restructuring, merger and relocation facilities which lead to massive redundancies, the development of social protection based on solidarity and free from commercial and economic interests, would be the immediate priority if it were employment that was really at the heart of the european union's concerns and not the concern to "give the golden pill" to deceive workers and limit them to reactions to its anti-social and unpopular economic policy directed against work. if we were to improve the living standards of workers, increase their purchasing power, guarantee employment stability, we would re-launch public and social investment, strengthen equal opportunities, fight against all forms of discrimination affecting access to employment and labour relations, block policies based on privatisation, low wages and social protection deficit, flexibility and precariousness of work, the constant exclusion of women, young people and disabled people from the labour market. but what economic and social policy guidelines promote, what is actually aimed at for workers, is a minimum poverty threshold, not a threshold of prosperity. prosperity is destined for big capital, to which we open a bright field of glory, leaving it with ever greater license, further deregulating the labour market in the name of increasing productivity and competitiveness, attacking ever more brutally the conquests and fundamental social rights, consecrating the conception that the modern (anti-social) state must provide only a minimum for the peoples, in terms of charity. the victims of longterm unemployment and social exclusion, numbering over 60 million, and the european workers as a whole, have no intention of being "adaptable", "solidarist", "docile" and "cooperative" in such choices, as you anxiously try to convince them. you will find them before you, uncompromising, refusing to make any compromises or concessions, fighting and demanding stable full-time employment, a social policy system guaranteeing security, health, pensions, protection for the unemployed, genuine equality of opportunity, quality education and sound vocational training, you will find them fighting for a social, democratic, solidarity europe, a europe of the workers, of the peoples. there you will find us, too, fighting alongside them.
- (el) mr president, today's session on human rights could be described as a solemn one. some, with ulterior motives, might say that europe is beating itself for its mistakes. i, for my part, believe that europe is renewing its hopes and renewing its prospects. the question of human rights is a prominent chapter and the foundation par excellence of european development. and we must not compromise on such a matter. we ask the applicant countries, the other countries, all parties to meet various criteria. for example, the death penalty. however, there is an allied country, the united states - i mention this as an element of international derision - in which the death penalty is in force and applied. i say this because if we are to build a europe based on the principles of humanism, it must never make concessions out of political opportunism but must show maturity and be forward-looking. the four reports we are voting on are good. they call for certain improvements and i think that the whole of parliament will be involved in this without opportunism or ulterior motives. mr belder's report, in particular, on the candidate countries, is a very good piece of work and i hope that it will be completed in an even better form. the temptation to improve quality leads me, in order to participate and contribute to an agreement, to read out two paragraphs, two proposals which concern two candidate countries. for cyprus: cyprus fulfils the copenhagen criteria but, by the way, very little progress has unfortunately been made on the question of the political partition of the island. for turkey: the commission report shows no progress on the kurdish question, which is fundamental. in short, the country still does not meet the copenhagen criteria. we are in favour of turkey's membership of the european union. but we must not compromise principles for anyone.
- (el) mr president, the hesitation about adopting the eurodac system has rightly raised a number of questions, both about the appropriateness and the effectiveness of this system for recording the fingerprints of asylum seekers and migrants, as a complement to the dublin convention. i am personally one of those who are very reserved about the system. already, several of its elements are raising our concerns about possible violations of the european convention on human rights and the protection of privacy, as well as the un international convention on the rights of the child, since it goes so far as to require fingerprinting of children from the age of 14. apart from this, this fingerprinting system gives rise to unfavourable associations and leads to the investigation by the police authorities of criminal acts and civil offences. in other words, the extension of the system to asylum seekers automatically equates all refugees and immigrants as a group with crime and illegality, which i consider to be profoundly unfair and alien to any notion of international law and respect for human rights, as they are prescribed by the geneva convention, the 50th anniversary of which we commemorated this morning with a lively debate in the european parliament. furthermore, the application of the system does not guarantee 100% certainty of identification, as has been shown in many criminal cases, and therefore cannot be considered to facilitate the implementation of the dublin convention and to prevent abuse of the right to asylum. in any event, the european parliament asks to be given the opportunity to exercise its control on the day when the council and the commission take a final decision on the matter. however, i believe that the best thing for the commission and the council to do is to adopt a common asylum and immigration policy, always based on european humanist and democratic values and respect for human rights.
counsel. - (pt) as everyone knows, mr president, in the context of the energy sanctions against the federal republic of yugoslavia, it was decided, on a proposal which was discussed in the council, to promote certain exceptional measures for the benefit of municipalities represented by democratic forces. this was a measure intended to send a message of support to the democratic forces and to serve as an encouragement precisely for those who are committed in the republic of yugoslavia, despite all the adversities, to adopt a behaviour and an attitude more consistent with the defence of democratic rights and the positions that are in the best interest of the yugoslav people. this programme started with two opposition-run localities. the european union then decided, on the basis of proposals from various member states, to extend this scheme to five other municipalities. this is a complex process, one which involves considerable budgetary costs, and the commission has made a considerable effort to find ways of extending these actions. we hope that the democratic forces in serbia will understand this signal from the european union. we deeply regret that we have to maintain the energy sanctions regime applied to yugoslavia, but it is part of the package of measures identified as being able to exert a form of pressure on the regime and, especially, a form of pressure on the way in which mr milosevic is running that country. it is obvious that this affects the civilian population. we are aware of this, but we must understand that the political situation in yugoslavia and the international behaviour of mr milosevic justify, in the council's unanimous opinion so far, the maintenance of this type of sanctions.

- (el) mr president, i will mention a concrete point to show that this issue may end up posing a very serious problem for the european economy and society. i am referring to the european origin rule for footwear which was adopted with the agreement between mexico and the european union. there is a specific agreement between the commission and the footwear industry under which the european origin rule is to apply, with a view to improving the competitiveness of the footwear industry at world level. unfortunately, in the agreement with mexico, the opposite has happened. another rule of origin is observed: that of mexico. and this, by definition, is to the detriment of the european footwear industry, which is therefore doomed to suffer a loss in its exports to mexico. the result is an export quota of 865,000 pairs of shoes from the european union to mexico. in other words, for a population of 90 million mexicans, we have managed to export to mexico one pair of european shoes for every 100 inhabitants of that country. we do not see it as a brilliant success. i'd say it's the opposite. i would like to express the dismay of the small and mediumsized enterprises in the european union working in the shoe sector: we cannot solve the problem by pretending that this is an exception to the agreement. the exception to the rule of origin concerns 15 000 smes in europe and 650 000 workers. i hope we will not have many such exceptions, because the problems we already have will only get worse. i would like the commission to ensure that this exception in the agreement with mexico will not be repeated, nor will it be seen as a precedent for new exceptions in other agreements. the commission must understand that if the precedent set by the mexican exemption is repeated, 50% of the european footwear industry will be threatened with extinction. i would like to know if this is how the commission sees its real support for smes, which we call the 'backbone' of the european union? is this how we are going to fight unemployment? i hope that such a mistake will not be repeated, so that the world of smes and the workers of the european union will not have to pay the price again.

- (el) mr president, it is obvious that we have asked this question because regulation 925/99 on the reduction of the noise level of aircraft is due to enter into force very soon, while in the meantime discussions are continuing between mrs de palacio and the american side, which is displeased with this regulation, which has been approved by the council and parliament, and is asking for its withdrawal, its postponement. it is clear that this regulation was adopted to protect the environment and citizens. however, at the same time it seems to be damaging to the interests of american industry. as a good faith gesture, the council and parliament postponed its entry into force for one year. the americans, despite our positive attitude, have shown their arrogance, particularly towards the european parliament, by first demanding the withdrawal of the regulation and then, with a small concession, talking about postponing it to an indefinite date. mrs de palacio, as we know, has been given a mandate by the council of ministers to negotiate with the american side. i have no doubt that these discussions, which were long, were also difficult. then one day it appeared that a little progress was being made, although it was certainly far from being considered satisfactory, but this little progress itself was short-lived because, in recent days, the american side, under article 84 of the chicago convention, brought an action against the european union before the icao because of this regulation, which, as i said, is intended to protect the environment. we want to make it clear that we do not want to cause tension with the americans, we do not want a trade war, we do not want problems in our relations with them, we consider them to be allies and friends, but they must understand that, as they themselves would say, 'it takes two to tango'. our position is as follows: - one, our strategy must be to achieve a global agreement in the sense of much higher standards for aircraft noise; - two, if no agreement is reached, it becomes clear that the european union is obliged to act unilaterally; - three, we consider the us position on withdrawal of the regulation or postponement of its entry into force to an indefinite date totally unacceptable; - four, we welcome the progress made in the aviation environment protection committee towards the phasing out of noisy aircraft in circulation today; - five, we are opposed to a further postponement of the entry into force of the regulation for aircraft of member states, because we consider this to be an internal matter and we do not believe that the americans have the competence to take measures concerning our own aircraft; - sixthly, we are prepared to review regulation 925/99 for aircraft from third countries, but only if, in the meantime, agreement is reached within the icao on new standards. this agreement, with the help of good faith, can be made a reality. but until then, there is no need to grant a deferral for third country aircraft, for the very simple reason that the regulation concerning them will enter into force in 2002. in this respect, i cannot understand the persistence of the americans who keep talking about postponing our regulation. in any case, we believe that the americans must abandon the action they have brought before the icao under article 84 of the chicago convention, because this action is destroying the climate of cooperation. i would like to conclude, mr president, by making two more specific remarks. first observation: it must be clear who is negotiating on the american side. we know that it is mrs de palacio, and she has been an excellent negotiator so far. on the other hand, it is not clear, so we do not know exactly what the position of the americans is, which complicates our efforts. secondly, the americans must understand that the european parliament is no longer the assembly it used to be in the past decades. it has become a strong institution. they must therefore understand that, in addition to the council, they also have to discuss with parliament and take parliament's positions very seriously, because without it no progress is possible. if they want us to help them, they must first help us, help to move things forward, show good will. i would ask the commissioner and the council to state their own position on this.

commission - (pt) mr president, the commission is fully aware of the need for action at european level, both coordinated and multidisciplinary in nature, both to prevent and to combat trafficking in women, as it has amply demonstrated in its two communications to the european parliament on this subject, the first in november 1997 and the second in december 1998. the commission has supported and will continue to support various non-governmental organisations in this sector in various candidate countries, and it has recently financed two prevention campaigns which we consider to be successful, one in poland and the other in hungary. the same type of campaign was funded in parallel by the us government as part of the transatlantic agenda for the prevention of trafficking in women in ukraine and bulgaria. in the field of police cooperation, the commission recalls that europol received a mandate from the council in 1996 to combat trafficking in human beings, with the fundamental objective of enabling the repression of trafficking networks active in europe. as regards criminal matters, the commission reiterates its intention to submit to the council and the european parliament, by the end of the current six-month period, a set of legislative proposals aimed at going further than the joint action of february 1997 and implementing the provisions of paragraph 48 of the tampere european council conclusions, with a view to adopting a framework decision on the common definition, prosecution and punishment of trafficking in human beings. finally, the commission also stresses that it has financed, through the stop and daphne programmes, numerous cooperation and training projects in the field of combating trafficking in human beings and in particular trafficking in women for the purpose of sexual exploitation, involving all the important actors in these programmes: police and judicial authorities, non-governmental organisations and social services. in the year 2000, the commission intends to present to the european parliament a new legal basis for the stop programme, covering the period 2001-2005. with this new legal basis for the stop programme, it will also be possible to finance projects to combat trafficking in human beings presented by non-governmental organisations and public authorities in the candidate countries. finally, as regards the specific situation in albania, the commission acknowledges that trafficking networks of people from the balkan regions are a cause for concern given the increasing number of cases reported in european countries. in this sense, we recognise that the fight against trafficking in human beings should be given priority in the framework of the stability pact for the balkans. i can even tell the honourable member that a group of experts is already working on identifying the best actions to combat human trafficking networks and that i myself had the opportunity recently, during a visit to greece, to exchange my impressions with the greek minister of the interior so that i could count on the collaboration of his government in defining these actions and priorities, given the action that greece is already taking bilaterally with regard to human trafficking in the balkans.

- (el) mr president, we are witnessing a great increase in the applications of biotechnology, and their importance is growing for the economy and for society, because the development of applications of genetically modified organisms will be decisive for the improvement of the quality of human life. therefore, it is essential to establish a modern framework governing the development, marketing and exploitation of genetically modified organisms. europe cannot lag behind the united states and japan. but that doesn't mean we're going to neglect the security that has to surround all these innovative applications - public health security and environmental protection. i should point out that we have recently been witnessing a constant war of statements about the safety of genetically modified organisms, which is a source of confusion among consumers who are not able to get an accurate idea of the benefits and risks of biotechnology applications. we must therefore win the confidence of consumers, and this will certainly be possible through measures to improve safety. for example, the prohibition of the creation or use of genetically modified organisms with genes that confer resistance to antibiotics, and objective and complete information to officials and the public, are such confidence-building measures. for this directive, parliament had requested amendments at first reading which met the two objectives mentioned above, namely a useful set of rules for the deliberate liberalisation and trade of genetically modified organisms, accompanied by safety measures for the protection of public health and the environment, and provisions providing for information for the public and consumers. it is also important to provide for the adaptation and amendment of the directive in the light of the data which will result from the rapid evolution of biotechnology and its applications. in conclusion, mr president, i would like to return to a point which i stressed in the committee on the environment and which deserves our special attention: it concerns the question of safety. in this respect, we expect the european commission to come forward with a more general proposal on the responsibility and safety that must surround a product. we must avoid excesses if we want this promising sector to be encouraged and supported by the directive under consideration.
the rapporteur. - (el) mr president, we all know that the crisis caused by bovine spongiform encephalopathy - the mad cow crisis - is a crime that has not yet been punished, and i doubt that it will ever be. in addition to this serious problem, we have also inherited a crisis of institutions, a market crisis, a fall in sales of bovine products, a fall in prices and a crisis of consumer confidence - which is the most important. the regulation under consideration comes late, very late in my view, but it finally comes to enable us to provide some answers, some solutions, to take some measures to restore consumer confidence in this area. as you will recall, mr president, we fought a battle last december over this regulation to preserve the rights of the european parliament. and we have also fought a battle to speed up the implementation of the provisions of this regulation as much as we can. anyway, we all know what he's talking about. on the basis of this regulation, very soon, in the course of 2000, all cattle will normally have to be identified and followed up to slaughter. the commission has already proposed that the european parliament should be consulted on the matter. we have tried, mr president, in analysing this regulation, to combine a large number of requirements and points of view. we have tried to simplify the provisions of the regulation as much as we can so that they are applicable - and applicable quickly, as quickly as possible. let us not forget, mr president, that the system we are about to set up is extremely complex and technical, and that it concerns about 100 million animals throughout the european union. we are talking about an extraordinarily complex device. the system must therefore be simple and applicable, it must be understandable to consumers - which is our main concern - and above all it must not provide for derogations, or 'windows', as they say, in other words possibilities of infringement through complicated formalities and derogations and applications for local or private use. i think we have succeeded, mr president. the system, at least the one which parliament is trying to establish by its amendments, can be applied from 1 september 2000, and we insist on this, because it was a requirement of the whole parliament that there should finally be dates, deadlines for entry into force. i would just like to stress two amendments, two categories of amendments, mr president. firstly, your rapporteur is respectfully asking that we have a report, a commission proposal, within a year, to ensure that this system also applies to meat products. if we are to be serious, we must ensure that consumer confidence extends to meat products, which, as we know, are very numerous. and secondly, there are amendments concerning the procedures for granting authorization to companies or anyone, traders, etc., to apply labelling, marking of meat. the provisions proposed by the commission are, in my view, far too flexible. the person concerned submits an application and, after a certain period, the system he wishes to apply enters into force. i propose that we reverse this system. for anyone to mark meat, it will be necessary to obtain formal approval from the competent authorities. it seems a little more onerous, but we already have a system, and in any case public approval will be required for meat to be marked, otherwise i fear, mr president, that the system will become lax, not to say degenerate, and in that case the main objective, which is consumer confidence in the system, will not be achieved.
the rapporteur. - madam president, ladies and gentlemen, with all due respect to the opinions of the members who have already spoken, i think it is very hard and unfair to consider our report inadmissible because of paragraph 15. other members have tabled amendments to vote against paragraph 15, and we can agree with those amendments. it is fair and democratic to agree or disagree with a paragraph in a report, and that is why we are here. but because of one paragraph, to ask that the report be declared inadmissible does not seem to me to be right. i ask that the motion for a resolution be voted against. we must examine and vote on the report during this part-session, because this is not a vote in favour of the two rapporteurs, it is a vote in favour of the opinion of the european parliament.
the rapporteur. - (el) madam president, mr minister, mr secretary of state, mr commissioner, ladies and gentlemen, we are facing a really important turning point in the evolution of the european union. the european union is called upon, through the procedures of the intergovernmental conference, to respond to the great challenges which mark the passage from the 20th to the 21st century, major challenges which we all know, which influence the course of the union every day, challenges which, above all, raise a fundamental question: to what extent are we, as an institution, as the european union, prepared to face them? the intergovernmental conference is trying to answer precisely this question. it is common ground to say that the european union, as it functions today, in the face of all these challenges, absolutely calls for certain transformations, transformations whose main aim will be to make it evolve further on the basis of everything we have all accepted so far. the importance of the report before you today lies in the following points. the first point is to seal once again the institutional nature of the union. we repeat it emphatically: the european union is both a union of states and a union of peoples. and it was precisely the acceptance of this principle that led him to the successes we can speak of, as we all surely do from time to time. the second point is to reaffirm and enshrine another important principle, that of the balance of power, a principle which relates precisely to the role and relationship of the members of the union, a principle which is expressed in the way in which all the member states express themselves within all - i repeat, all - the institutions of the european union. and as you know, madam president, ladies and gentlemen, the european union has reached its present position and its successes precisely because it has operated and continues to operate on the basis of an agreed and consensual balance of power. the third fundamental principle enshrined in the report is precisely the proposal for a general - i stress 'general' - review of all the institutions which make up the european union. perhaps this is too risky a proposition for some. but i must stress that it is necessary, indispensable, not least because, first of all, we all agree that the current review of the way the european union works is directly linked to the great challenge of enlargement. and it is only natural that we should all see and agree that in a europe of 15 member states there are already dysfunctions that need to be remedied, all the more so when we are talking about a union of 18, 20, 25 and 27 members. the fourth point concerns certain new proposals, although the issues to which they refer have always been the subject of discussion. more specifically, i want to talk about enhanced cooperation. this, at least as we conceive it, must have two elements. the first is that it must be an incentive and not an obstacle - not an obstacle, i insist - an incentive for all member states to participate more deeply in european procedures. the second element is an addition of democratic design, which means that if one or more countries, for whatever reason, wish, at least initially, not to participate, this cannot be considered as an exclusion or a limitation of their presence within the union. we have made a lot of progress and we have at least two examples of enhanced cooperation: the schengen agreements and economic and monetary union itself. for my part, i would like to see enhanced cooperation extended to foreign policy issues and other areas. to make myself clearer, i will say that enhanced cooperation is a community procedure in which questions of common foreign and defence policy must be included, because there are control mechanisms. i would like to conclude by thanking the portuguese presidency, and in particular mr da costa, for the cooperation we have had, commissioner barnier, you and all your colleagues and, of course, the co-rapporteur, mr leinen, for the hard but very good work we have done together, as well as the members of parliament's secretariat who have assisted us and the interpreters who have often exceeded the time limit to enable us to conduct the difficult negotiations you envisage.
the commission is also proposing to extend the scope of the directive to include the use of the term 'common carrier' in the context of the directive. - (el) madam president, the proposals of the committee on women's rights for the intergovernmental conference are aimed at strengthening democracy and efficiency. while the amsterdam treaty has been a great step forward for women's rights, providing a solid basis for effective equality policy, it unfortunately does not go beyond the professional sphere. we therefore propose to introduce into the treaty a single and coherent legal basis for gender equality, covering all policy areas. we declare that the balanced participation of women and men in decision-making at all levels is a prerequisite for democracy and social justice, and therefore we call for the amendment of the relevant articles of the treaties so that this principle is taken into account in the composition of the commission, the european parliament, the court of justice, the court of first instance, the court of auditors, the economic and social committee and the committee of the regions. we also stress the need to establish a close link between the intergovernmental conference and the process of drawing up the charter of fundamental rights, so that, in the context of the constitutionalisation of the union, the fundamental right to equality between women and men is also incorporated into community law. therefore, in addition to the balance and cohesion that is indispensable to ensure between large and small countries in the union, between developed and less developed regions and population groups, it is also necessary to take gender equality into account in shaping the future of the european union and in decision-making.
- (el) mr president, with the dimitrakopoulos-leinen report under consideration, the european parliament is setting out its grand design for the future of the european union. the moment is indeed historic, as has been emphasized. our fellow citizens are waiting to hear what we are going to ask the intergovernmental conference to do about this future. institutional proposals on specific issues, without first clarifying the philosophy which will underpin the future union of 27, are not convincing. the motion for a resolution rightly defines this basic concept in three principles. firstly, according to the proposal, the european union is a union of peoples and a union of states. its citizens therefore have a european and a national essence. secondly, the union is based on an institutional balance of large and small states. it can't just be on paper. thirdly, the union must become more efficient in order to accommodate the great enlargement. the philosophical principles set out above, to which the motion for a resolution remains faithful, are not metaphysical inventions of the european parliament but derive from the history and political reality of what is called the 'european space'. all those who do not know this are building europe on sand. the intergovernmental conference for the year 2000 is being held to make the union as effective as the great enlargement that has been decided requires. parliament has a very great responsibility in this process. parliament's approval of enlargement can therefore only depend on the adequacy of the institutional reforms that will be adopted. this is not a threat but an expression of responsibility. all the groups in this house are working in good faith to make the functioning of the european union more efficient by means of amendments. but is it enough that procedures are simplified and shortened to be considered effective? the effectiveness of a police service, which indeed requires simplicity and speed, cannot be confused with the effectiveness of the fundamental institutions of a democratic legal order. the effectiveness of democratic institutions is not measured by the clock, nor by the number of decisions that are adopted. it is measured by the degree of legitimacy it establishes. the dual legitimacy conferred on the european union by the european citizen and the national citizen, by the peoples and the states, makes it inherently complex at institutional level. the simplification and shortening of procedures is therefore limited by the completeness of the complex system of dual legitimacy. that is what we must bear in mind tomorrow when we vote on the amendments and the text.
in writing. - (el) the lisbon summit, which can only be euphemistically described as the 'employment summit', despite the loud declarations that accompanied it, only resulted in a new step towards the conversion of employment into employability, the redefinition of the notion of 'worker', the deregulation of labour relations and rights, in order to best serve, for the benefit of big capital, the famous 'new economy', the last avatar of the old economic and social liberalism that we are familiar with, but in an even more extreme and implacable form. under the ambitious title of 'modernising the european social model by investing in human resources', the demands of big business to increase its profits are presented with the greatest cynicism as 'employment support measures'. strengthening the competitiveness of enterprises by reducing labour costs, full liberalisation and flexibility of the labour market, support for atypical forms of employment, reform of social security systems and provocative elimination of the last vestiges of the welfare state. this is not an 'investment in human resources' as the summit claims. it is an alienation of man for the benefit of the market, a subordination of workers to financial requirements and criteria, a subordination of the welfare state to the indicators and criteria of nominal convergence and emu. what the lisbon guidelines for economic and social policy promote, what is actually prepared for the workers and "served" to them under a deceptive "modernization" - in order to "make them feel like it", to abuse them and to limit protests against the eu's economic and social policy directed against the workers and the people - is a poverty threshold and not a threshold of prosperity. the "new economy" is feverishly preparing the creation of a vast layer of "employable poor", with poverty wages, with rights to insurance and sickness benefits amputated, forced to change jobs at the behest of an unbridled market and laws dictated by the illegal interests of very large capital. the eu does not seem to be interested in fighting unemployment, promoting and supporting employment stability and full employment. on the contrary, it is hastily preparing workers for life in a regime of full and permanent precariousness. in the conclusions of the presidency, it is boldly stated that the aim is to 'improve employability', and the information society and education are put at the service of this logic, hand and foot bound, as instruments for strengthening parttime employment, remote work and work on demand. prosperity is reserved for big capital, which is faced with a magnificent field of glory, since, in order to satisfy its demand for a "fully functioning internal market" and greater competitiveness vis-à-vis the united states, efforts are being made to fully reflect the pressures of international competition on the labour market, to accelerate the liberalization of the so-called "structural transformations" (privatizations, market liberalization) of vital sectors such as energy, telecommunications, post offices, transport, water supply, etc. the restructuring, merger and relocation of firms, which result in tens of thousands of redundancies, is being facilitated rather than regulated. public and social investment is constrained, equal opportunities and the fight against all forms of discrimination in access to employment and labour relations are systematically undermined, and priority is given to everything that serves the objective of the 'economic return' of labour for capital, in other words low wages, the social protection deficit, the flexibility and precariousness of work, the exclusion of women, young people and disabled people from the labour market. the victims of longterm unemployment, mass poverty and social exclusion, numbering over 60 million, but also the european workers as a whole, cannot be "adaptable", "solidarist", "submissive" and "cooperative" in making such choices. you will find them before you, uncompromising, unyielding and unyielding, fighting and demanding a stable, full-time job, a social policy system including health and old age insurance, protection for the unemployed, genuine equality of opportunity, quality education and sound vocational training. the only point in the lisbon declarations that could be agreed upon is that 'human resources are europe's most valuable capital', except that they are not willing to be 'coined' on the international financial markets and sacrificed to ensure that monopolies can increase their profits. it is this capital which will prove to be valuable in the struggle for a social, democratic and united europe, a europe of peace, progress and socialism.

counsel. - (pt) mr president, the council has already raised with the turkish authorities the question of the democratic rights of turks of kurdish origin, including those mentioned in the question. membership of the european union at the helsinki european council. this status puts it on an equal footing with any other candidate country. when we talk about equal footing, it means for better and for worse. in other words, turkey is now subject to the same requirements as any other candidate country for membership of the european union, but it is also, of course, acquiring the same rights. the european union cannot make turkey a special case, it must be treated exactly the same as any other candidate. turkey's situation is therefore now being assessed against the criteria of the copenhagen european council, which are sufficiently clear as regards respect for the rule of law, democratic guarantees, fundamental freedoms and the principles of protection of minorities. this is a prerequisite for the opening of any negotiation process. the commission, on behalf of the union, and turkey must decide on a preaccession strategy in this context. the non-governmental organisations, counsel. - (pt) mr president, the objective of the european union, in accordance with security council resolution 1244, remains the creation of a democratic and multiethnic kosovo within the internationally recognised borders of the former yugoslav republic. the council agreed on the urgent need to find a solution to the outbreak of ethnic violence, in particular against the serb community, the roma and other non-albanian communities in kosovo. the council fully supports the efforts of minuk and kfor to prevent further evictions and to allow the safe return of all those who have been forced to leave their homes. in response to a pressing appeal by the special representative of the secretary-general, mr bernard kouchner, and the high representative, mr xavier solana, member states have significantly increased their contributions to the un international police force, in terms of financial and technical support as well as human resources. various member states have reinforced their own contingents in kfor. i would remind you that we have had occasion in this parliament in the past to express our deep regret that these commitments have not been respected, which obviously makes it more difficult for the international authorities to act in kosovo. today there are already new elements and some of these commitments have been met, which allows for a different treatment, a more efficient treatment of these types of issues, although we are still far from a minimum acceptable solution. the council urged all political leaders in kosovo to adopt urgent measures to combat the escalation of violence, to reject forms of extremism and to support unmik and kfor. we think it is very important that the council can extend the progress already made in the reconciliation of the ethnic groups and that it is possible to implement resolution 1244. we are engaged in a process of democratization and coexistence in kosovo. only a sharing of responsibilities between representatives of minorities can lead to the construction of a multiethnic kosovo. we must all ask ourselves - and i would like us all to be aware of this - whether everyone, especially inside kosovo, really wants a multiethnic kosovo. in this context, the council again calls on the serbian leadership to participate in the interim joint administrative structures. the council attaches the greatest importance to the careful preparation by unmik and the oecd of the local elections which are expected to take place this year, including the provisions applicable to absentee voting and minority participation. we believe it is important to start creating legitimate local power structures in kosovo: this is the only way for us to ensure that the political forces that are expressing themselves today in an unorganised and uninstitutionalized way return to normal political life and eventually express themselves in kosovo within the framework of a democratic society. in order to support this process of democratisation and cohabitation, the european union has worked closely with other international partners to reconstruct the daily lives of all the people of kosovo, regardless of their ethnicity. in addition to the substantial bilateral assistance provided by the member states, the european community provided humanitarian aid worth eur 429 million in 1999-2000 to the region for kosovo refugees, internally displaced persons and persons returning to their homes. reconstruction and extraordinary financial assistance, including unmik support, will total eur 437 million for 1999 and 2000. this set of actions and the commitment shown by the european union in this area are recognised by the international community. unfortunately, sometimes the practical effectiveness of these actions is not enough to put an end to an imbalanced situation and to the points of tension which we hope will be reduced in the future.

counsel. - (pt) security council resolution 1244 gives the authority over the territory to the unmik, the provisional administration of kosovo. it provides for full legislative and executive powers, as well as the administration of the judiciary, to constitute a provisional administration with a mandate that is described in the resolution itself. this also includes the development of a phased economic recovery plan. therefore, the individual cases mentioned by the honourable member in his question will have to be dealt with directly with the special representative of the un secretary-general for kosovo, who is himself responsible for the administration of that territory, or with the minister for foreign affairs of the member state in which the companies have their headquarters. the council assumes overall political responsibility for the european union's participation in unmik, which is concerned with economic reconstruction and rehabilitation. however, neither the european union as such nor the council has direct competence or responsibility for the administration of the territory and the protection of the individual interests of the enterprises in the region. this is the responsibility of the united nations for the administration of the territory and the governments of each member state present on the ground for the protection of the enterprises.

in writing. - (el) the citizens of europe, and more particularly the workers, farmers, employees of smes, women, young people, the new poor, all those who suffer from eu policies, are already demonstrating, in a more or less combative way, their dissatisfaction and their disapproval of the choices made by the european union. unfortunately, the resolution for the intergovernmental conference, instead of paying attention to the anxieties of all these social strata and supporting their demands, responds directly to the calls of big capital and adopts the choices of the monopolies which are in favour of the militarization of europe, of interventions - even military - in third countries, of a greater restriction of national independence and of the popular power of each country. the eu, after the single act and the maastricht and amsterdam treaties, is now moving, under the pressure of enlargement and the new order, towards the examination of questions which could not be resolved at the previous intergovernmental conferences. despite the denials, we are faced with an integrated plan of attack by big capital against the democratic, social, collective and individual rights and freedoms that the workers have won with their hard fights, within the framework of bourgeois democracy. imperialism, the multinationals are using the european union as a battering ram to break the resistance of the peoples, to divert the struggles they are waging for a better future. the efforts, supposedly undertaken in the name of democracy and 'the efficiency of institutions', serve mainly the interests of the monopolies and can only meet with the opposition of the workers, the peoples of europe. what does the generalization of majority rule, with the majority of the states and people of the eu as the limit, mean if not to disinterest oneself completely and to minimize the interests of the "smaller" countries, the less developed regions, the weaker social strata and, more generally, the workers and popular strata of the eu? what else does the abolition of the right of veto that a country has the possibility to oppose when its vital national interests are seriously affected mean? what does the cyclical alternation of the nationalities of the commissioners and the non-alternance of the eu presidency mean if not to keep a so-called smaller country out of the centres of power for long periods of time and thus essentially to deprive it of the possibility of serving its interests and those of its people, to the extent, of course, that this can be done within the framework of the eu? what does limiting the number of meps, despite the increase in eu member countries, mean if not to silence the voices that hinder big capital and the realization of its plans, to promote political processes that will prevent the existence of any alternative proposal and will be reduced to insignificant and harmless alternations of power for capital? why is the role of national parliaments being weakened, thus severely limiting any democratic control? why is the weighting of votes in the council changing in favour of the big countries and to the detriment of the small ones? all this and many other things which are included in the european parliament's vote and which the members of the european parliament from the greek communist party will vote against, as well as elements contained in the recent commission proposals, can only lead to one conclusion: the peoples of europe cannot expect anything positive from this new intergovernmental conference either. they must take their own interests into their own hands and defend them on their own, through their own struggles. the eu will always find struggles in its path, more and more energetic, more and more dynamic, despite its attempts to deceive, to throw dust in the eyes or to sugarcoat the pill. struggles which will lead the peoples of the eu to coordinate their action, to form an all-powerful pan-european popular front against the eu and its policies, for another europe without oppressors, without exploitation and without war.

in writing. - (el) the continuation of the same policy, applied more strictly and extended to new areas, is the strategy envisaged by the commission for the internal market for the next five years, as is clear from the communication it has made on this subject. the european parliament's report not only supports this approach but also calls for these measures to be accelerated. the 'huge profits of the internal market' are the starting point of the proposed objectives, as it is characteristically said. however, it is not at all clear who benefits and who is victimized by this process. there is no doubt that all the measures which constitute the materialization of the principles of freedom of establishment and freedom to provide services, the measures of market liberalisation, the measures for the free movement of goods, and the extensive legislation relating thereto, only contribute to strengthening the large multinational companies for which the possibilities of expansion and action in other markets have been facilitated at a considerably lower cost and which have been the beneficiaries of the deregulation measures, which have enabled them to increase their profits and consolidate their position. unfortunately, this is not the case for small businesses, or even for medium-sized businesses in small member states, for which the dramatic intensification of competition has resulted in an increase in the number of bankruptcies, reductions in their activities and staff. of course, the problem for these companies is not "the obstacles to exploiting the opportunities offered by the internal market", but precisely the measures taken to make the internal market a reality, the principles on which eu competition law is based, the fall in demand among the broad layers of the population, a consequence of the anti-people austerity policies. and, of course, the solution is not in venture capital and money markets, which, given the limited resources of small businesses, exacerbate the dangers faced by most of them, but in an alternative economic development policy that would support productive investment, increase demand and facilitate the activity of small businesses. the report also calls for new facilities for businesses, through the "simplification of the legal, administrative and fiscal framework", as well as "legislation that would not impose burdens on or reduce the competitiveness of businesses vis-à-vis foreign competitors". in short, more freedom for the action of capital, more limitation of legislative requirements. on the other hand, nothing is said about workers' rights, because a greater "lifting of barriers" leads to a reduction in the protection of labour legislation, greater freedom in the procedures for mass redundancies, mergers and relocations. it is not by chance that neither the specific proposals of the commission nor the guidelines of the european councils refer to the need to limit these phenomena, to control speculative movements of capital and to impose fiscal and other measures on it. the report calls for even greater liberalisation in areas such as the pharmaceutical market, the reduction of the tax burden on employment, thus proving that the measures taken are aimed at whetting the appetite for ever greater liberalisation of the unrestrained action of capital and profits, with extremely dangerous consequences for the whole social protection system. in this respect, the report in question is entirely in line with the anti-people economic and social policy in the context of the single market and emu and, of course, we will vote against it.
