the rapporteur. - (el) mr president, i should like to begin by saying how pleased i was to hear mr fatuzzo's explanation of vote, which i wholeheartedly endorse. anyway, let's get back to the serious stuff. mr president, madam vice-president of the commission, the decision of the essen european council of 1994 on the transeuropean transport networks, in conjunction - i should like to stress this - with the joint decision of the council and the european parliament of 23 july 1996 on the community guidelines for the development of the transeuropean network, was considered at the time as one of the most important steps on the road to genuine european unification. the construction of modern motorways and high-speed rail networks that would allow safe and fast travel across most of the member states, the construction of modern international ports and airports that would facilitate the mobility of people, goods and services between the various member states, as well as with third countries, were rightly considered both as an indispensable complement and a prerequisite for the smooth functioning and success of the single market that had just come into being. and there could be no internal market without a vast system of transeuropean networks. however, now, almost five years after the launch of this process, it is clear in practice that the achievement of this ambitious objective faces many problems, the first and most important of which is the lack of a sufficient level of funding. as i note in my report, it is particularly worrying that for seven of the 14 priority projects in essen, sufficient funding has not yet been secured or even a definitive timetable for implementation agreed. and of course it is well known that the funding from community funds is far below the real needs and that it is in no way sufficient to carry out the projects. but that is not his main objective. direct community aid for the construction of the buildings represents only a small part of community financing. the latter is mainly intended to finance feasibility and technical studies, as well as interest subsidies and loan guarantees. in other words, the funding is intended to support and complement the measures taken by the member states, which are also responsible for the bulk of the funding for the construction of the trans-european networks, and not just for the actual implementation of the projects. on the other hand, the concerns and difficulties of the governments of the member states are understandable, especially when one considers the restrictions on public spending which they were virtually obliged to resort to in the 1990s in order to meet the maastricht criteria and achieve their integration into emu. but there is a problem. and it must be admitted, mr president, that there could be no transeuropean networks without funding. that is why the main proposal in my report is perhaps to increase the budgetary appropriations allocated to the construction of the transeuropean networks. this is a question which the commission and, above all, the member states will have to examine. the second proposal we make is to strengthen cooperation between the public and private sectors in the construction of transeuropean networks, because we believe that the public sector, while having the same amounts, can do more work in this way, that is, by mobilising private sector capital. and i would like to stress the important role of the european investment bank in this respect. a third proposal is to reduce the share of the community budget devoted to studies on transeuropean networks from 47% to 30%, so that some resources are made available to subsidize the construction of networks and to grant loans. the fourth proposal aims to introduce measures and incentives for the seven problem networks and, above all, to establish a timetable for implementation, and we invite the commission to cooperate with the member states concerned to this end. a fifth proposal is for the commission to give greater weight to the forthcoming revision of the guidelines for trans-european networks with a view to improving access for island and peripheral regions to the central regions of europe. i think i will be forgiven for being particularly sensitive to this aspect, coming from a region which is both insular and peripheral. another proposal in my report calls for close cooperation between the commission, germany, austria and italy on the brenner tunnel, a communication project of vital importance which is naturally part of the transeuropean networks. we also believe that the principle of efficiency should be emphasised, by encouraging alternatives to new investment, by promoting new technologies and intelligent transport systems such as telematics for road users and new signalling for rail networks. finally, we ask the commission to provide more information on financing by the peripheral member states and the private sector in its future annual reports. and since it is, of course, the member states and regions that are responsible for providing this information, we invite them to provide the commission with the relevant data. - (el) mr president, according to the commission's proposal, the first stated objective, which our rapporteur, mr sterckx, agrees with, is to increase the liberalization of air transport and its total subordination to the rules of an uncontrolled market. the commission says that this will lead to a reduction in airline expenditure, improved passenger services and more innovation, and our rapporteur agrees with this. as a result, it is proposed to cede airports, with all their strategic importance for the security of a country, to multinational capital, despite the unpleasant consequences that will result, and this by promoting the pursuit of maximum profit by the private owners of airports. the commission is proposing to transfer to the european union the sovereign rights of each country to control its airspace, with a view to creating a single system of aviation safety, but at the same time it is calling for the privatisation of operational air traffic control services, which will then, it says, have competence over the whole of the european union's airspace and will compete with each other. this proposal, which goes beyond the abolition of the sovereign right of each country in its airspace, will create confusion and have a negative impact on flight safety, since, objectively, competitiveness, which is also promoted as an asset in this area, will be used to ensure the greatest possible profit and not the greatest possible safety of flights. it is also argued that this whole scheme will reduce flight delays and, consequently, the costs of airlines. that may be true. but there is no doubt, as we have seen in similar cases, that if we do increase profits, it will not translate into lower rates or better services. instead, we will have a deterioration. it is also proposed to abolish state aid to airlines, which will have disastrous consequences for air links in disadvantaged regions. we have the very typical example of greece. the undeniable fact is ignored that, as the experience of official workers' organisations and unions in the field of air transport shows at international level, the steady decline in the cost of operating aircraft and ground services, combined with widespread privatisation and the pursuit of maximum profit by private capital, is the cause of the continuing increase in air accidents. for all these reasons, mr president, we shall vote against the report.
- (el) mr president, with its proposals to the council of ministers, the commission does not fix, as it falsely claims, the prices of agricultural products and aid, but in our view it merely recalls the prices and aid which will be in force for the 2000-2001 marketing year in accordance with the existing regulations and, at most, modifies certain monthly increases in intervention prices for all products to which this system still applies. however, we would like to point out, firstly, that producer prices and aid are frozen for many agricultural products for the 12th consecutive year, as is the case for sheepmeat and goatmeat, while for other products there is a concomitant decline resulting from the constant increase in the cost of production, which has the effect of reducing net agricultural income, as is shown by the rest of the eurostat data. the question is: what are farmers doing about this? secondly, the degradation, or even the abolition, of intervention leaves the farmer at the mercy of the impunity of traders and industrialists who buy from the producer at ridiculous prices and often sell at much higher prices. thus, while even nominal producer prices have fallen, often to the point of collapse, consumer prices have risen. in other words, we have a spoliation of the producer and at the same time a spoliation of the consumer, in the name of the protection that the european union claims to provide them. the question is what farmers and consumers will do about it. will they intensify their struggles? thirdly, the low quotas and the resulting crushing fines are further reducing the aid for the 1999 harvest. so aid has become a gift with no value. a striking example is the oil problem, which also concerns my country and my homeland, lesbos. the aid for 1998-99 was announced at drachma 460, but the producers received drachma 330 a year later. the same applies to cotton: its minimum price is 101 euros per 100 kilos, but the greek producer will receive less than 65 euros because of the co-responsibility. the commission has already made a number of proposals to the council, and i am sure that the council will be able to accept them. we must also say that this will further weaken the intervention system, since it will also be abolished for cereals, as it has been for other products. we also disapprove of the payment of the monthly increase in november, with the harvest taking place in greece in june. similarly, mr president, we propose that intervention should take effect from 1 october or 1 november, not 1 april, because then our producers are at the mercy of the speculative bulimia of traders and industrialists for six months. - (el) mr president, if we have learned anything from the bovine spongiform encephalopathy crisis, it is that health has the highest priority over all other policies. this is the basis for the development of the regulation under review and should be the basis for a systematic effort to prevent, control and eradicate transmissible spongiform encephalopathies. the problem has been known for years, but unfortunately, even today, we have not been able to eradicate the disease and identify all the causes that lead to its manifestation, so that concerns, limitations and disruptions in the production and marketing of products of animal origin result. the community as a whole must therefore take measures to ensure that, first, public health is effectively protected and, second, consumer confidence is restored, which in turn will contribute to the growth and proper functioning of the internal market. one of the important measures proposed in the regulation is the introduction of rapid diagnostic tests, as early detection is a crucial point in the fight against the disease. the commission has already taken a number of measures to ensure that the community's financial resources are used to the full. three tests have already been proposed today, and we expect that in the future we will have improvements in this area as well as rapid diagnostic tests in live animals to better track the epidemiological status of transmissible spongiform encephalopathies in the european union. a second measure is to launch a permanent epidemiological study which will be based, inter alia, on laboratory tests carried out on the basis of a defined protocol. moreover, the adoption of uniform procedures and the perfectly comparable results of the analyses carried out by the national and community reference laboratories will help us to carry out close monitoring and to obtain reliable scientific data and diagnostic methods which will also include rapid diagnostic tests. in addition, it would be useful, at the same time, to follow the evolution of the scientific record of the new form of creutzfeldt-jacob disease, which, as is known, affects humans and is caused by food from animals contaminated with bovine spongiform encephalopathy. finally, any suspected spongiform encephalopathy in an animal should be reported to the competent authority so that we can take appropriate action. in this connection, i would like to emphasize that i am of the opinion that, in order to effectively eradicate the disease, the entire herd must in fact be slaughtered, mr president, despite the economic and emotional cost of this measure. the compensation to be paid by the commission to the beneficiaries must therefore be substantial. it is very difficult, when one animal is infected in a herd, to distinguish whether there are others, apparently healthy, who could later contract the disease, and this is why this measure is proposed. "prevention is better than cure", said hippocrates many years ago, laying the foundations for preventive action in health, especially since there is no treatment for bovine spongiform encephalopathy or for the manifestation of this disease in humans, namely the new form of creutzfeldt-jakob. in conclusion, i would like to congratulate and thank mr böge, rapporteur for the committee on agriculture, and mrs roth-behrendt, rapporteur for the committee on the environment, because i really believe that we have a much better regulation, thanks also to the cooperation and assistance of all the other members, and that the text thus revised far surpasses the one which the commission had originally submitted to parliament.
- (el) mr president, the purpose of the proposal for a regulation under consideration is not to tackle the problems, as the commission hypocritically claims, but to cover up the problems that have countries into categories. if the commission really wanted to protect public health against spongiform encephalopathies, it would have long ago proposed rules to eliminate such risks, which it has not to give the impression of a certain control. the conclusion drawn from this draft regulation is that it is rather a matter of confusing the situation. this is the result of the country classification process, where instead of taking into account the basic criterion of whether or not there are cases of spongiform encephalopathy among their farmed cattle, other aspects are taken into account. but will be banned. we therefore oppose the commission's proposal.

the rapporteur. - (el) mr president, the resolution of the committee on economic and monetary affairs submitted to parliament's plenary session contains a very positive overall assessment of the commission's recommendations on the general guidelines of the economic policies of the member states. the commission has once again worked efficiently and diligently and is backing its proposals with impressive econometric analysis. let me praise it publicly. the recommendation for the year 2000 was made in the context of positive developments and optimistic prospects for the progress of the european economy. the prospects for national income growth in europe, exceeding 3% in 2000 and 2001 - 3.4% according to the latest estimates for the year 2000 - are well above the average rates of growth that were really disappointing throughout the 1990s. these forecasts therefore provide a window of opportunity, provided of course that it is realized. they form a foundation that will launch europe's economy towards higher growth rates in a consistent and environmentally compatible way. it is worth noting that such performance is a prerequisite for improving the employment situation and for strengthening europe's competitive position in the world economy. the economic committee accepts that the required growth rate is best expressed as a gdp growth rate of 3% or more; the commission has also taken the view that this rate cannot be sustained without a substantial increase in the production capacity ('potential product') of the eu economy. moreover, it considers that the increase in production capacity presupposes profitable investments. but the commission is careful to avoid specifying the share of these investments in gdp. the economic committee is of the opinion that the definition of quantitative economic objectives, while useful, must not jeopardise the objectives laid down in the stability pact, which are the spearhead of economic policies in the euro area. despite all these reservations, the report of the economic committee expresses its satisfaction at the fact that the commission's recommendation repeatedly refers to the quantitative objective defined in the conclusions of the presidency of the european council in lisbon. this objective is to increase the employment rate from 61% to 70% by 2010 and to increase the number of women in employment from 51% to over 60% by 2000. the economic committee argues that the required economic growth must be based on a regime of price stability, on budgetary discipline, on wage increases in line with the criterion of price stability, productivity growth and a fall in unit wage costs - even if a reduction in working time were to come into effect. it also believes in greater liberalisation of competition, consolidation of national competition authorities, strengthening of sme associations and strengthening of the single market - particularly in the area of public utilities and financial services. as regards the latter in particular, following a broad consensus, we will ask, through a joint amendment, for the financial services project to be implemented more quickly in 2003 instead of 2005. the majority of the committee believes that this market stimulus will lead to the necessary increase in investment by market means. however, at the same time, while it approves of the reorientation of public expenditure towards investment which must be justified - as far as possible - by the cost-benefit ratio, it rejects public investment on the grounds of simple business cycle management. the commission also welcomes the fact that the european council has adopted a new strategy for the fight against poverty, which aims to promote the development of a more inclusive and sustainable society. however, it is concerned at the gap between the major challenges and the often reduced recommendations for concrete action. it therefore asks the commission and the council to take into account the need for a radical overhaul of the intellectual apparatus which produces the general guidelines. the economic and social committee once again stresses and insists on a longstanding request by parliament for the conclusion of an interinstitutional agreement between the council, the commission and parliament on the adoption of this resolution, and thanks the portuguese presidency for its diligent collaboration on this subject. however , this is ad hoc cooperation and not the product of an interinstitutional agreement; in our opinion, this is a flaw in our procedure. ladies and gentlemen, we have reached a broad consensus in our work in the economic committee. i would like to stress that the goal of full employment for the next decade is entirely achievable and on this we all agree. of course, there are differences of opinion on the best way to achieve these objectives. some of us feel that the setting of quantitative targets - even in the absence of a mechanism to make them mandatory - would help europe to put its efforts towards achieving the required targets into practice. this is particularly true of the investment objective, which is a necessary element for raising the potential growth rate of the economy to 3% on a permanent basis. we know that rate. the commission has repeatedly defined it in its studies; it would not have been harmful to include her. just as one could have expressed more categorically the idea - which i think is widespread - that a new start is needed for the transeuropean networks and that we must face the need for their financing, which comes from the combination of private capital and public investment. we could have tried to give the public sector a somewhat more positive role than that of confining it to a role of 'watchdog' of price stability, without in any way giving the impression of wasteful public consumption. there is room for all these actions in the window of opportunity, if the main danger to our economy comes precisely from the possible crisis in the us financial markets, an event that could lead to the immobilization of the private investment mechanism. i would like to ask the commissioner whether he is aware of the fact that the commission has not yet taken a decision on the matter. the european economy has ample room for growth; it is true that the european economy can become more productive, but this implies greater boldness in our future approaches.
- (el) mr president, the commission's recommendation on the broad economic guidelines for the year 2000, although it allows for a return to full employment, retains the basic features of previous years. in recognition of the contribution of parliament and our rapporteur to the return to the concept of full employment, i think the rapporteur himself, whom i commend for his efforts, will agree that we need to redefine what we mean by 'employment' and especially by 'full employment'. what do we mean by 'workers' when we are faced with a policy that dismantles labour relations, flexibilises employment and, through this process, makes extreme poverty massive? as regards the general approach, the commission's recommendation is part of the overall neoliberal economic and social policy which, with the support of the majority of the economic and monetary affairs committee, is also reflected in the report under consideration. in our view, the following should be fundamental policy elements: support for public investment, stable, permanent and full-time employment, protection and improvement of the social insurance system and, more generally, of the social protection system, strengthening of effective demand through improvement of the purchasing power of workers. this is where the broad guidelines should be. the amendments we are proposing to the european parliament are in the same direction and we invite our colleagues to vote for them.

appropriate nor socially just. the rich got richer, and the poor got poorer. with the country's entry into emu, we're taking the plunge, and saving who can.
in writing. - (el) the fact is that man's strength and ability have their limits. working hours can affect not only the physical health but also the moral health of individuals and families. it must therefore be organised in such a way that the safety and health of workers is not endangered. yet in the european union, seven million transport, fisheries and medical trainees are excluded from the scope of directive 10493ec on the organisation of working time. the unacceptable conditions and abuses affecting working time and workers in the above excluded sectors are of such a nature and scale that it is no longer possible to postpone the imposition of rules when there are workers, such as doctors in training in britain, who work up to 72 hours a week. the extension of the directive is therefore essential, but it is not sufficient. the proposed provisions are not able to protect the safety and health of workers effectively, since there are still many derogations and long delays are foreseen for the entry into force of the directive. and let us not forget that the original directive did not fix the length of the working day (it fixed a weekly duration of 48 hours - hence the suspicion that the measure will be circumvented).

counsel. - (pt) we know that the situation in northern iraq, and in particular the lack of control by the authorities in baghdad over the north of the country, is creating a political and even military vacuum, which has led to concrete actions by turkey. this is a situation which is essentially due to the uncertain situation in this area. in this respect, it is clear that the european union takes a negative position on all acts which do not respect international borders. i cannot say more on this subject, except that we are still concerned about the territorial integrity of iraq and that any infringement of that territorial integrity is undesirable.

the rapporteur. - (el) mr president, commissioner, ladies and gentlemen, the use of the radio spectrum for the transmission of signals has brought about a real revolution in telecommunications. in the decades that followed, new applications using the radio spectrum continued to develop. we have now gone from simple wireless telegraphy to digital transmission of sound and images, satellite navigation and the third generation of mobile telephony. in this respect, i would like to stress that telecommunications are not the only use of the radio spectrum. beyond the applications based on the transmission and reception of the radio signal, there are also so-called passive uses, which consist solely of capturing signals produced by natural phenomena. the main uses are radio astronomy, which studies natural phenomena outside our planet on the basis of their emission on radio waves, and earth observation which uses the radio spectrum. the ever-increasing demand for radio frequencies for new applications naturally leads to a review of the principles on the basis of which they will be allocated and the policy to be followed by the european union in this field. in response to this need, the european commission launched a public consultation on the green paper on radio spectrum policy in 1998. the european commission then published a communication, which we are now examining, based in particular on the public consultation mentioned above. the committee on industry, foreign trade, research and energy, after welcoming the work done by the european commission, makes a number of observations and formulates certain principles. recognising that spectrum is a vital and scarce natural resource with a growing demand, it stresses that its use must be efficient in order to best serve the public interest and contribute to the development of new high-quality services. in this respect, the industry committee considers that spectrum policy cannot be exclusively market driven and that the economic and social value of spectrum use cannot be assessed on the basis of profit opportunities, but that commercial interests and the interests of society should be balanced. the industry committee also stresses that concrete measures must be taken to ensure a sufficient number of frequency bands for public and private broadcasting, for amateur radio, for navigation systems and for passive uses such as radio astronomy and earth observation. the industry committee accepts the european commission's proposal to create a spectrum policy expert group with a purely advisory role, transparent internal structure and operation. however, while recognising that some further harmonisation of community radio spectrum policy for pan-european services and applications would be desirable, it does not endorse the european commission's proposal to establish a regulatory framework at pan-european level, which it considers premature and hasty. i would like to draw your attention to a question which has raised a lot of questions in the committee on industry - mr paasilinna has mentioned it earlier - namely, to what extent the principle of charging for the use of frequencies should be adopted, including the auctioning and the creation of a secondary market. the position of your rapporteur is that radio frequency cannot be a transactional object in itself, because this would lead to dissociating the allocation of frequencies from the use for which they are intended. moreover, practices such as auctions and secondary markets will lead with mathematical precision to the domination of the most powerful economic interests that will monopolize the market. your rapporteur agrees with the payment of a flat-rate price for each commercial service using the spectrum. the industry committee did not share my view and, while not fully endorsing the auction, considers that it may be suitable for commercial use. i would point out to my colleagues that the results of the recent auctioning in the united kingdom of radio frequencies for third generation mobile telephony provide an opportunity to reconsider the question. in fact, the huge sums invested in the auction will not come from the companies' profits but will be borne by the users. i have heard that in the united kingdom, to cover the amount paid in the auctions, every man, woman and child will have to pay £400 a year in telephone charges. consider the consequences of such practices for the end user. i therefore ask my colleagues to vote for amendments nos 4, 12, 5 and 6 so that our final decision contains an explicit position against the auction.
commission. - mr president, i would like to congratulate mr paasilinna and mr alyssandrakis on their very timely and invaluable work. the fifth report, which is part of an ongoing monitoring exercise by the commission, sets the stage for many of the conclusions and recommendations made in the review of the communication which was published at the same time and which we are currently working on in collaboration with the european parliament. the report by mr paasilinna inevitably addresses some of the problems which will have to be dealt with in the final proposals, which we will be debating again in this house next month. the commission shares to a large extent the concerns expressed in mr paasilinna's report regarding the current regulatory situation. these are vital to stimulate interest in the information society, the development of the internet, e-commerce and mobile commerce. they shall provide separate access at the local loop level, facilitating the development of licences, co-positioning, pre-selection of the carrier and the independence and competence of the national regulatory authorities. the commission is currently dealing with many of the problems you have mentioned. it has the recent recommendation on the local loop for clustering. the commission will also exercise its competition powers to monitor closely dominant positions. we have also drawn up a revised recommendation on interconnection pricing and are currently implementing enforcement measures and infringement procedures, for example against several member states for carrier preselection and local pricing structures. pending the adoption of this new framework, which will be presented in june, the commission will continue to insist that the current rules be applied, as market players, national regulatory authorities and national administrative authorities are calling for. in addition, the commission was also unanimously mandated by the lisbon summit. the commission is currently preparing the sixth framework report, which is due to be adopted in the course of the year 2000. the report by parliament will make an important contribution to this. i am convinced that the report will be an important reference point when we follow the evolution of the information society. wired systems will be examined more closely by the commission. we have already adopted a policy of encouraging the abolition of cable television networks in order to ensure greater local competition and to stimulate investment and services, as parliament has called for in the reports. as regards the issue of radio spectrum and mr alyssandrakis' report, i share the view that radio spectrum is a resource which is becoming increasingly scarce and valuable. spectrum is used in many policy areas that are essential for the eu, such as telecommunications, broadcasting, transport, research and development, all of which are of vital importance to the european economy. the commission therefore proposes a harmonised approach. mobile and wireless communications are developing at a rapid pace and the surprisingly high results generated by the auctioning of spectrum licence rights in the uk is a testament to the growing importance of this sector. in addition, the messages of the green paper on radio spectrum policy demonstrate that there is a real need to address some of the issues related to radio spectrum at community level and to achieve a balance between different user groups. secondly, community action on radio spectrum will only have positive effects if it is carried out within a clear regulatory framework. in addition, political support is needed to facilitate the achievement of the community's objectives at international level. a world conference on radio communications will be held in istanbul where issues such as galileo and the third generation spectrum will be discussed. the commission therefore welcomes the report by mr alyssandrakis. the report mentions the need for a radio spectrum policy which is in line with community policies, and further harmonisation of radio spectrum is necessary. we must ensure the development of information and communication technologies and ensure that a balance is struck for all spectrum needs, including private and public interests. we must develop the competitiveness of european industry on a global scale in the interests of european citizens. the commission would also like to underline the urgency and not the premature need to establish an appropriate framework at community level in order to implement the spectrum policy options agreed in the spectrum policy group and to ensure that the results of the harmonisation mandates are implemented within the union. we should be able to promote harmonization measures which respond to technological and market developments without delay. the commission will submit an annual report to the council and the european parliament on the measures adopted. as regards additional measures, the commission will present at the end of june - after parliament's debate and the vote on the radio reports - the appropriate community framework for radio spectrum policy in the ec. it will also take into account the results of the overall discussion currently taking place in the context of the 1999 revision. we all understand the debate on auction prices, which has been of great interest to us in this house. under the new provisions in force, the commission should recognise the power of member states to choose the spectrum licensing method they prefer, whether it be an administrative or auction method. however, the commission will monitor developments in these methods closely to avoid distortions of competition and to ensure that the licensing directive is applied and that technologies and services develop in the interests of european citizens. mrs read asked what the balance was between competition and regulation. i would say that we need regulations to stimulate the development of competition, because otherwise bottlenecks could actually prevent the emergence of competition. competition can be beneficial to citizens, as it will provide an incentive to reduce prices and improve the quality of services. however, there are areas where we also see a policy of overriding public interest, particularly with regard to universal services and data protection. in these areas, the commission will present proposals in the context of the new legislation on electronic communications to be presented at the end of june. at the same time, the commission also intends to define this balance between markets and public policy in the context of the europe action plan, which is due to be presented next week. finally, as regards the consistency of licensing agreements, we must ensure that there is no unfair competition and we must ensure that the licensing directive is applied. the whole of this debate is of great importance for the definition of the content of the new legislation on electronic communications and i am convinced that these issues will not be the focus of the attention of a few passionate and expert members of this house as is the case today. electronic communications will become a major political issue in the years to come.
the rapporteur. - (el) mr president, commissioner, ladies and gentlemen, the launch of the first artificial satellite by the soviet union in 1957 marked the beginning of space exploration and at the same time opened a new era for mankind. the ability of man to launch spacecraft has pushed our limits far beyond the earth's atmosphere and has brought us a wealth of new knowledge about the solar system and the universe as a whole. at the same time, it has opened up the possibility of practical applications that have changed our lives. the use of artificial satellites for telecommunications, weather forecasting, earth observation and navigation is so widespread that it would be difficult to imagine the continuation of these activities without the use of space. the high technical requirements of spacecraft have led to enormous technological advances that have gradually entered our daily lives. finally, space has always been of strategic importance: whoever controls space also controls the earth. space is a paramount area where international cooperation is essential. at european level, the european space agency (esa), an independent body with 14 european countries, 12 of which are members of the european union, has played a crucial role in cooperation on space exploration, applications and the development of research from space. to date, the council, the commission and the european parliament have addressed space policy issues on many occasions. the main political issue emerging from the debate is the development of a coherent approach to space and the promotion of cooperation between the european union and asean, based on clear objectives and within a well-defined framework. the european commission working document under discussion today is a response to the council resolution of 22 june 1998 which called on the commission to take practical measures to strengthen cooperation between the european union and asean. this paper discusses certain aspects of the european union's space policy which relate to practical applications, mainly in the fields of telecommunications, satellite navigation and earth observation, without mentioning either space exploration or its use for scientific research. from this point of view, the scope of the document is somewhat restricted, since a comprehensive space policy cannot be limited to practical applications. the commission makes it clear that a coherent approach is essential for the european union. to this end, a consultative structure for space and related issues has already been established, with the active participation of esae. this structure brings together the space advisory group and other high-level groups. the european commission's paper concludes that the european union must discover new ways of implementing a space policy, and proposes well-defined guidelines. in its resolution, the committee on industry, foreign trade, research and energy strongly expresses the wish that space be used for peaceful purposes for the benefit of all mankind. at the same time, it considers that space policy cannot be dominated by the market alone and that long-term estimates of the benefits and risks of space exploitation should be taken into account, not only from the point of view of industrial interests but also for the well-being of mankind. it stressed the importance of international cooperation, the need for all member states to participate in space activities, the importance of cooperation with third countries, and noted that esa had been effective and had played a leading role in space research and applications, both at european and global level. the industry committee welcomes the european commission's intention to establish a policy framework for future programmes and stresses that this framework will have to be established following extensive and in-depth consultations between all interested parties. it favours further development of launch systems, the development of new telecommunications systems and the strengthening of european participation in the alpha station programme, and reiterates the european parliament's support for the establishment of a global satellite navigation system - known as galileo. it called on the commission to take, in collaboration with the member states, all the necessary measures to ensure the effective use of space systems to monitor the environment and ensure safety, to protect people's lives and natural resources, to detect at an early stage - and even prevent - natural or man-made disasters, and to help combat fraud. i am convinced, ladies and gentlemen, that the debate we are having today will help to develop space policy further.

countries where women still today have no place in the sun and are deprived of basic human rights. this does not mean, of course, that we underestimate the progress that has been made and the contribution that the european union has made to the dynamic presence of women in all professional and scientific fields, the performance of women at all levels of education, their successes wherever which reflects the changes that have taken place, which addresses the problems and removes the contradictions resulting from an archaic gender contract. the european union and the member states will have to be at the forefront of this fight.
in writing. - (el) although the objective pursued is to increase production, investment and employment within the union, the commission's broad guidelines for the economic policies of the member states and the community contain a series of negative directions: the maintenance of budgetary restrictions, beyond the obligations of the stability pact; wage limits; tax reform in favour of the powerful; the further decomposition of the labour market; the paramount importance attached to competition policy, which undermines the principles of social justice. the committee on economic and monetary affairs, despite the sensitivity of its rapporteur, our colleague mr katiforis, has retained this spirit of the commission and for that reason i shall vote against the motion for a resolution. the union must promote a policy which combines quantitative development with the reduction of working time, the increase of productivity, tax reform in a social and development direction, and modernisation, not the abolition of the public sector.
in writing. - (el) the european parliament's legislative proposal simply accepts the proposal of the european commission, which merely states that greece fulfils the conditions necessary for the introduction of the single currency. the abolition of the derogation for greece will take effect from 1 january 2000. formally, greece does indeed cover the financial and monetary indices required for emu entry. in this context - taking into account the positions of my party, the union of the left and the progress towards greece's entry into economic and monetary union - i voted in favour. nevertheless, i personally express my objections and reservations in the strongest possible terms to the emu policies which trap the member countries in unyielding financial austerity, lead to the uninterrupted increase in unemployment and the widening of social disparities, unbridled competition, without, however, offering a strong european currency which can, on a global scale, face the dollar. i also express my total opposition to the way in which the greek government has promoted our accession to emu, by setting a nominal convergence rather than a real one as its objective, by making greece one of the countries in the union with the highest unemployment rate, by continuously selling off public sector assets in order to cover the treasury deficits. unfortunately, these policies do not end with emu, but are instead institutionalised, stabilised and made permanent. the progressive and leftist forces in the emu countries must extend their cooperation in order to achieve a radical reform of the way in which emu is built, to assert the priority of social criteria and the fight against unemployment, to encourage truly convergent policies, sustainable and fair development policies.
in writing. - (el) the european parliament is today called upon to give an opinion on greece's adoption of the single currency, after the commission and the european central bank gave the green light. first of all, we would point out that for us the question is not technical at all, but deeply political. it is precisely because of the ideological and political aspect of the whole process of emu and the single currency that we will vote against the drachma's entry into the euro area. from a technical point of view, there is no doubt that greece fully meets the criteria laid down by the maastricht treaty for entry into the third stage of emu. the problem, however, is how we got here and what will happen in the future. the completion of the nominal convergence criteria has required heavy sacrifices on the part of the greek people and has been the result of an inflexible austerity policy, the main features of which were and are the reduction of social spending, an extremely restrictive fiscal policy, the freezing of wages, the limitation of workers' protection, the liberalisation of markets and the encouragement of privatisation. unfortunately, this policy, which has brought broad social strata of our people into a desperate state, has led to a dramatic increase in unemployment, the accelerated extinction of the rural population and the deindustrialisation of the country, which will not only not be reversed with the entry into the monetary union, but, on the contrary, will worsen. the obligations imposed by the stability pact, as well as the strong recommendations that accompanied the reports of the commission and the european central bank, bear witness to this. both demanded the continuation of the austerity policy, the acceleration of the de-structuring of labour relations, rapid measures to overturn the public insurance system, the continuation and acceleration of privatisation. it is clear that not only do workers have nothing to gain from this single currency, which is being acclaimed with great deceptive shouts, but on the contrary, they are sure to suffer, under even stricter conditions and under the ever-increased "high control" of the eu, the same policy of austerity without end, conducted over the last ten years. moreover, the voices that arise in favour of the need to ensure the sustainability of the maintenance of the criteria are aimed precisely at facilitating the hardening of the policy pursued so far and at supporting the particularly anti-popular measures that are on the horizon. the experience of other peoples, who are already in the euro zone, shows that, step by step, the gains of the last decades are being destroyed and, immediately, the "american model" is now directly constituting the example to be followed on which the famous european social model will be shaped. the european members of the greek communist party, faithful to the positions they adopted when the maastricht treaty was signed, and against which only the greek communist party voted when the greek parliament was called upon to ratify it, will vote against greece's entry into the euro area, motivated by concern for the interests of the workers and of our country. at the same time, we will continue to fight alongside the workers against this policy, in order to achieve an alternative development proposal which will be centred on man and his needs, a proposal which will be the antithesis of the reactionary policy of the 'democracy of monopolies'.
in writing. - (el) despite some positive elements, the report by mrs plooij van gorsel is imbued with the idea that research should be put at the service of business, produce new knowledge on demand and, of course, knowledge that is commercially exploitable, in the context of market liberalisation and competition. any research policy based on such concepts, serving such policies, will alter the character of research, as a process of producing new knowledge, but also as a productive force. what research needs most is more funding, which is unacceptably low for the eu as a whole (1.8% of gdp compared to 2.8% in the us and 2.9% in japan), and in countries such as greece, which ranks last in the eu, it is at the tragic level of 0.5%. the increase in funding will provide the opportunity to develop new research activities and to employ the highly qualified young researchers, currently unemployed, who are numerous, at least in my country. funding should come from public bodies, as part of a research programme that will strengthen both basic research and applications whose ultimate aim should be the improvement of the quality of life for all men, not just monopolistic profit. international cooperation plays a key role in research, and further development of such cooperation, both between eu member states and with other countries, is desirable and welcome. however, the commitment of all research efforts in the eu countries to the service of big business, with the sole objective of increasing its profits, is not desirable and is, on the contrary, a discouraging prospect for the future of humanity. for these reasons, the members of the european parliament from the greek communist party vote against the report by mrs plooij van gorsel.
commission. - (el) mr president, i think it is important, in order to facilitate the debate, to inform the members of the way in which the commission has implemented parliament's proposal with regard to the adoption of the amendments. everyone knows that, as the commissioner responsible, i submitted a package against discrimination a few months ago, which contained two directives and a programme. today we are debating one of these two directives and let me say, ladies and gentlemen, that this debate is possible thanks to the admirable cooperation and the particularly effective work of the two committees of this parliament whose efforts, i believe, have exceeded all expectations. a directive which is extremely complex, with multiple consequences, which has national specificities and calls for different points of view, different contributions from all the committees which took part in it, has managed to become a report and, moreover, to attract such support. i would like to congratulate mrs buitenweg and mr howitt, all those who have worked on this, and the portuguese presidency. i believe that this sitting is historic for two reasons: firstly, because the request for such a legislative act has been on the table for more than twenty years. social groups, political parties, parliaments, european parliamentarians, international conferences have proposed and insisted on the legislative coverage of this great issue of racism and the attitude of organized societies. twenty years later, it becomes an act. we are debating this directive. the second reason is that, because of the political situation, because of the political problems which have arisen in the union in recent months with regard to cases of racism at the political and social level, our work today is the first to get rid of words, resolutions and general political formulas and to implement a concrete policy. the european commission, after the report of your committee, has worked very hard, in collaboration with all the departments involved, and i have to tell you that more than half of the amendments have been accepted, either in spirit or in part in their wording. i should even point out that the amendments which were not approved were not approved mainly for administrative or legal reasons or because we considered it impossible for the council to adopt them, after the experience and cooperation we have had all this time. as you know, we rely on article 13; this article is a restricted article; on this basis, we can move forward. i would like to comment on some amendments. i should like to say that we agree with the amendments concerning the incorporation of the gender dimension (amendments 7 and 59), those concerning the scope of strictly personal application (amendments 5, 30, 31, 34, 36 and 37), and those concerning the implementation of positive discrimination (amendment 40). we also welcome the idea of conciliation procedures referred to in amendment no 42, the dialogue with non-governmental organisations (amendments nos 21, 50 and 51) and the amendments relating to earlier acts of the european parliament and the council (amendments nos 3, 4, 8, 9 and 12). i also agree with your proposal to give greater importance to the role of the european observatory - which we will include in amendment no 59 - although i must point out that, in the light of the commission's experience, we consider that submitting reports every two years is a very demanding task for the member states and will not allow us to properly assess the response of developing policies. you also propose to change the definition of the term 'indirect discrimination', as one of the speakers has already pointed out. i agree with the points in the amendment, but i must stress that we will use them in such a way that they help to ensure that the wording of the definition is as close as possible to that of the concept defined by the court of justice of the european communities in the o'flynn case. i would like to take a moment to clarify the article on the rebalancing of the burden of proof proposed in amendment no 43, although its acceptance, as has emerged in this debate, but also in the council, will pose a serious problem. however, i would point out - since the commission agrees with this approach - that a similar debate was held a number of years ago on the same logic, on discriminatory treatment between the sexes. already at that time there was great concern about the impact not only on the courts, where it was feared that too many cases would be concentrated, but also on businesses. after many years of application, we must admit that these fears were unjustified and that no problems arose. i believe that, with this experience, we can dare to take such an approach. i would agree - and i commend your persistence - that the directive should apply to all persons living in the territory of the member states and not just to community citizens. i also agree with the proposal to clarify that the directive will concern natural and legal persons, but i must stress that what you say about groups of non-institutionalized persons cannot be taken into account, because this concept is not legally enshrined. the content of the concept is understandable, but since it has no legal basis, it will cause many problems. i would also like to address a matter which seems to be of particular concern to the house, namely racial and ethnic segregation on the one hand and religious segregation on the other. during the preparation of the proposal, there was a wide-ranging debate within the commission on the need to maintain the exclusion of discrimination on grounds of religion in the directive in question. we are aware of the frequent difficulty in clarifying this discrimination. this is often the case in the work environment, as problems arise with regard to the possibility of praying, of granting leave for religious reasons, of having specific food available at the workplace. for this reason, the second directive of the package, on employment and discrimination at work, makes very clear reference to religious discrimination. however, we understand how the question can become even more delicate and complex if we have an extended ban on grounds of religious discrimination, for example in education. and how thorny the negotiation of the directive would be with the member states, where there are diametrically opposed approaches. we welcome, however, the approach you have taken in pointing out in amendments nos 15 and 29 that some may be trying to interpret racial discrimination as discriminatory treatment on the basis of religion; i therefore share your view that the directive on this point needs to be strengthened and that the member states must clearly draw the attention of the judicial and other competent authorities to this danger, as regards the treatment of the issue of the right to appeal against discrimination. i would like to make a brief reference to the independent bodies: i agree with some of the ideas you have expressed about their role and, in particular, the need for their independence, but i must say that we cannot go into detail about their organisation and functioning, because this concerns the member states themselves. the number of amendments we have actually accepted is very large and i think we will agree on the need to look at how these ideas will be incorporated into an amended proposal so that the structure and balance of the text is not altered and the proposal has a chance of being accepted by the council, because i believe that for all of us the perfect balance is between what is desirable and what is possible. i shall mention very briefly the most fundamental amendments which cannot be accepted. the first concerns your reservations about the fact that certain services, such as the police or the judicial authorities, are exempt from the detailed draft report. as the rapporteur has very clearly pointed out, the directive can only be implemented where there is community competence. this cannot apply to judicial cooperation in criminal matters or to police cooperation, both of which fall within the scope of the treaty on european union. we therefore agree with the reference to public bodies in general in amendment no 37; but i cannot accept, in particular, the specific reference to the police and the civil law authorities. a note on the huge issue of the particular problems in the area of migration and asylum policies: due to the great complexity of the issue, the commission prefers to take a step-by-step approach and, in the course of developing specific legislative means for admission policies in these two areas, asylum and immigration, the commission will assess the situation and consider to what extent the inclusion of an ad hoc clause against discrimination would be the appropriate response. there are other elements, such as public commissions, for which the framework is prohibitive and does not allow the introduction of such criteria, the question of statistical information also, for which i admit and emphasize the rapporteur. - (el) mr president, my report is in fact about the proposal for a regulation submitted by the finnish presidency to regulate the relations and obligations of the member states for the readmission of third country nationals within the european union. i am deeply sorry to have to say, on behalf of the committee on civil liberties and internal affairs, that the proposal for a regulation has not received the attention, study and careful preparation it should have received, which is required to deal with a delicate and complex matter. as a result of this lack of preparation, the european parliament and the council, the relevant committees of the european parliament, namely the committee on civil liberties and the legal affairs committee, as well as the non-governmental organisations which the finnish presidency does not seem to have informed, were forced either to express serious reservations, to reject or to abandon the proposal for a directive. the tampere conclusions have demonstrated the need for a comprehensive approach to the phenomenon of immigration, which is of course linked to a long series of unanswered policy questions. it is linked to respect for human rights and to the economic, social, political and cultural conditions of the countries of origin. on readmission, member states already have experience with an agreement concluded in the framework of european political cooperation and schengen. this is a sensitive issue that requires cooperation between pillars. as regards the content of the finnish initiative, the draft proposal is not accompanied by a statement of reasons. the initiative is not inspired by any strategic vision. the project is aimed at imposing a dublin system on illegal immigrants, modelled on the convention on asylum seekers, a convention whose application, as we know, has proved to be very problematic and ineffective. the proposal for a regulation gives priority to the return of aliens who are illegally present on the territory of the member states, through a system of compulsory readmission between member states. the regulation aims to determine the member state obliged to readmit the third-country national, thus allowing the implementation of the agreement. the draft regulation does not exclude the possibility that a member state may return to its country of origin or to another third country a foreigner who does not fulfil the conditions for entry or residence in a member state. this provision seems to legitimize even more the rigorous decisions that member states could take outside the framework of the proposed regulation. another point which is open to criticism is that the draft excludes any kind of solidarity between member states, particularly as regards illegal border crossing and responsibility for the external borders of the european union. the proposal may constitute a direct pressure mechanism to delimit the borders of the european union. the regulation also lacks any sensitivity to the personal situation of those who, before being classified as illegal immigrants, may have lived in the member state in question for a very long time. the regulations also exclude residence permits for asylum seekers, thus creating new opportunities to undermine their protection. the regulation allows for readmission to be requested from another member state within six months of the moment when the authorities of the member state requesting readmission become aware that the third-country national may be - not 'is' - in an irregular situation. the proposal therefore allows for the establishment of proof of entry into the territory of a member state on the basis of hypothetical elements. the way in which the elements essential for the identification of persons to be returned are defined is open to any transgression. however, the fundamental problem seems to be the absence of a european immigration strategy and policy. i believe that, commissioner, we must urgently move towards the preparation of the legal instruments for the immigration directive, rather than simply resorting to repressive and police measures which are not subject to any democratic control. the tampere conclusions need to be implemented urgently and progress made towards the development of a common european asylum and immigration policy, based on european humanist values and respect for fundamental human rights. in conclusion, we ask the european commission to take the necessary legislative initiatives in all matters relating to immigration and, of course, to determine the reciprocal obligations of the member states. we know your personal interest, commissioner, and we know the difficulties you are facing in the council, and we declare that we will support you unconditionally in all the initiatives you take in this direction.
the rapporteur, mrs elmalan, has already said that the vote on the motions for resolutions will take place at the end of the debate. if we want finland's initiative to be rejected, we must vote against it. that is what we vote on first. therefore, those who vote for, vote for finland's proposal, which was rejected by the committee. the commission has also proposed that the european parliament should be consulted on the draft directive on the protection of the environment.

in writing. - (el) no one disputes the harmful effects of smoking and no one can be hostile to measures to curb it. in this respect, there are provisions in the commission's proposal for a directive which are considered positive. but there are serious doubts about the possibility of reducing consumption with the commission's proposals, and it is certain that they will have very negative consequences both for the tobacco producers in the community and for the manufacturers and traders in the sector. the amendments concerning the reduction of consumption of products are strongly opposed, they are aimed at reducing budgetary expenditure (around €1 billion a year, whereas the tax revenues of the 15 eu member states from the tobacco sector amount to €60 billion a year), and their eventual adoption will cause huge and irreparable social, political and economic damage to the regional economies, which in the case of greece will be even worse. our country will also be in serious trouble if, as the commission proposes, the three-year transitional period for the implementation of the directive is waived, and if it applies not only to tobacco products consumed in the eu but also to those intended for export to countries where the strict provisions of the directive are not applied.
counsel. - (pt) mr president, the general question of the size of the community budget is a question that is dealt with cyclically, especially in the context of the wider debate on the future financing of the european union which precedes the negotiations on the financial frameworks of the european union. however, it seems that the general context is not favourable to strengthening the budgetary dimension, as the agenda 2000 negotiations have shown, and that the time is not right to reopen this debate. i would remind you that, in the context of the interinstitutional agreement of 6 may 1999 on budgetary discipline and improvement of the budgetary process, of which the financial perspective is an integral part, the amounts fixed represent an annual ceiling on expenditure for the three institutions, the european parliament, the council and the commission, which they have all committed themselves to respect. at the present stage, as i said, the council does not consider it necessary to revise the financial perspective. as regards expenditure arising from the enlargement of the agreement in question, it is apparent that, in the event of enlargement of the union to include new member states during the period covered by the financial perspective, the european parliament and the council, acting on a proposal from the commission, will adopt the financial perspective jointly to take account of the need for expenditure arising from that enlargement. this amendment to the financial perspective will, however, have to respect the ceilings of the indicative framework for the financial perspective of an enlarged union of 21 countries, as shown in annex ii to the interinstitutional agreement, subject of course to the results of the accession negotiations.

counsel. - (pt) mr president, the council has never been called upon to debate the facts raised by the honourable member. in principle, this is not a matter for the community but for the member states' authorities and, more specifically, as the honourable member points out in his question, for the greek high authority for data protection. as i have just said, no proposal has been submitted to the council in this area. if such behaviour by the greek authorities were to infringe community legislation, the commission, as guardian of the treaties, would not fail to take the necessary measures to bring any action before the court of justice for possible non-compliance. but as i have just said, this is not the council's responsibility, but the commission's. it is for the commission to assess whether the reference to religious affiliation on greek identity cards is in accordance with the principle of non-discrimination on grounds of religious affiliation. however, it seems to us, a priori, that the mere reference to religious affiliation does not constitute a breach of the legal and political acquis of the european union if it is motivated by information or even statistical purposes and if it is not accompanied by any type of concrete measures involving discrimination against the followers of a particular religion. we are in the field of action of free and democratic states: any type of rejection in relation to a measure of this kind has its specific legal framework of claim in the national context. it goes without saying that the community bodies can function as an appellate body for these same national bodies.
- (el) i thank the president-in-office for his reply. i have to say that i submitted a corresponding question to the european commission yesterday. i received the answer today. however, there is some confusion about this - a subject of great importance in greece. i thank the president-in-office, although i note a contradiction in his answer. firstly, it tells us that the question is not a matter for the community but for the member state. and secondly, it poses a problem of adaptation to the law. i assume that the president-in-office has clearly in mind the answer which the commission has given me on this point, namely that the mention of religion would be a breach of directive 46/95 on personal data. does the president-in-office agree with this answer, if he is aware of it? or is he unaware of it, which would then be evidence of a considerable lack of coordination between the council and the commission on a very serious matter?
- (pt) mr president, there is no contradiction in this matter and the problem is very simple: as the matter is not covered by any community rule, it is purely and simply within the greek domestic political order and greek laws. of course, this would no longer be the case if a community rule were to be transposed into greek law and thus became itself a greek law. the problem here is whether or not, at this stage of the process, this constitutes an infringement of the community legal order. only the commissioner has the authority to react to this matter. since it has not done so so far, this means that at this moment the question is not yet a community question. this question therefore has no community dimension at this stage. if it were to have a community dimension, the commission would be empowered to take the decisions it considers appropriate, in particular before the court of justice.
- (el) mr president, i am speaking without intending to do so, because there has been a misinformation circulating in the house that in greece, in order to become european, greeks will have to not mention their religion on their identity cards, as has been the case for the past 70 years in our country. the minister's reply reflects the reality. there is no provision for intervention, it is a matter of national law, and that is what the greek side has been doing so far. the authority which was set up for personal data has issued a decision that the mention of religion is a clear breach of the rules on personal data. but i know that the community directive which refers to this question states that if the person concerned agrees to the mention of religion, it is not forbidden to mention this personal data. and that is precisely what our party and the church of greece are demanding: the optional mention. whoever wants to mention it does, and whoever doesn't want to refrain from it.

in writing. - (el) the problem of asylum is a major political issue; it is a question of the fundamental principles of democracy, the guarantee of basic individual rights and freedoms, and the guarantee of human dignity. it is only in this light that the problem of asylum seekers should be examined and any attempt to harmonise asylum policy and convert it into a vehicle for reducing fundamental human rights, strengthening repressive and police measures and building a european fortress should be rejected. this should not be a pretext for intensifying and strengthening the electronic recording systems that are being set up in the framework of europol and schengen, which are directed not only against refugees but also against fundamental individual freedoms of eu citizens. it is essential to take all necessary legislative and other measures to respect the rights of asylum seekers, in strict compliance with the provisions of the geneva convention. it is essential to observe and strengthen all provisions on the protection and granting of asylum to all those who, individually or collectively, are persecuted in their countries by state, para-state or other groups for ideological, philosophical, political, religious, racial, cultural or other reasons, by granting them guarantees and conditions of reception and life that are dignified. asylum seekers in eu member states must be treated with dignity and humanity. careful consideration must also be given to all the parameters and specificities of their situation, the risks they face if returned to their country, and the necessary protection measures, even for those who do not meet the formal criteria for asylum - which must be clearly defined and in accordance with the principles of international law and fundamental human rights. arbitrary and brutal expulsions are frequent, due to the difficulty of the applicant to resort to the judicial authorities for protection, and collective expulsions are inadmissible acts in violation of human rights conventions and principles. we would like to point out that the further development of uniform criteria for the asylum system, gradually and concretely departing from those established by the geneva convention and replacing them, in essence, by provisions such as those set out in the report, can have a negative and repressive impact. in this spirit, we deplore the so-called 'burden-sharing' policy which conceals a clear attempt by some powerful eu states to get rid of the 'burden' of taking in large numbers of refugees and channelling them to other countries. finally, we are firmly convinced that the best and most effective way of dealing with the refugee problem is to create conditions of peace, social, economic and democratic progress in all countries of the world. as long as these conditions are not ensured and are undermined by powerful economic and military interests which destroy, at their will and for their own benefit, structures, societies, the rights and dignity of peoples, the waves of uprooted and persecuted people will increase.

- (el) mr president, there are no geographical, social or national limits to stopping and combating the scourge of drugs. therefore, whatever effort is made to study the phenomenon, it should not be confined to the geographical boundaries of the member states, even those of the candidate countries, but the data on which the study is to be based should concern as many countries as possible so that they can contribute more effectively to the prevention and combating of this problem. the reasons and processes which lead an ever-increasing number of young people, at an ever-earlier age, to resort to the world of evasion and drugs must be studied in depth. the collection, verification and processing of data from more countries will only contribute to an integrated study and to the search for effective solutions if this gathering and exchange of information is done in such a way as to cover the widest possible field and to obtain comparable data. for example, in my country, so far, most of the information we have is based on the treatment centres, without any use being made of other bodies which could help us to get a more complete and integrated picture of the scale of the problem. the treatment centres are used by young people who can access them, whether they are helped by their family or by the wider social environment. but what about a large part of the population that does not have such access, such as economic immigrants? according to the data of the hellenic centre for the treatment of addicts, 97% of the subjects who use its services are greek citizens. according to prison statistics, the majority of drug addicts in prison are economic immigrants who, due to language, lack of information and social exclusion, have not been through any centre. consequently, in the data collected and processed by the centre, this category of drug addicts is almost non-existent. in my view, every addict should have the opportunity to go to a drug rehabilitation centre without being obliged to provide full personal data. we know that the use of drugs leads to illegality, not because the use itself is illegal, but because the high cost of these substances, which is set by the huge economic sectors that promote them, leads with mathematical precision all drug addicts to illegality.
