- (el) mr president, the commission's report on monitoring the application of community law is one of the most important texts because it reveals the real situation in the european union, and perhaps we should devote more sessions to it, and certainly not just the one on friday. the rapporteur, mrs grossetête, in her particularly balanced work, makes a number of relevant observations which will have to be analysed on an ad hoc basis. both reports rightly raise the question of the application of community law at three levels: non-transposition of community law, incompatibility of transposition measures with the content of community law, and the application of the rules. as regards non-transposition, delays are still considerable, especially in certain areas, including the social field, which i regret to note is not mentioned in the commission's report, whereas the relevant issues are linked to social cohesion. the reasons for non-transposition are numerous and need to be analysed. at the same time, it is time to devise various policy instruments to reduce delays. we could consider designating a single body at a high political level in each country, which would be responsible to the european union for the correct and timely transposition of community law. we would add that each country that assumes the presidency must declare in time and publicly its degree of compliance with community law, since it is inconceivable that it should have the responsibility for the management of political matters without being consistent with its obligations. it would also be useful, perhaps, to publicly announce the compliance status of each country before each european summit. in the past, delays in transposing community law did not have the consequences they have in a single economic area where it is inconceivable that there is not, at the same time, a single community legal order. the question of the detailed rules for the application of community law is primarily linked to the national and community judicial system and to the requirements of the committee on petitions of the european parliament and the european ombudsman. the two fundamental problems with the judicial system are, on the one hand, the often unjustified referral of cases to the european court of justice, or the poorly prepared referral, as mrs álvarez mentions, and, on the other hand, the lack of referral of crucial questions. and these two problems, despite their apparent differences, have a common cause, namely insufficient familiarity of the national legal professions with community law. a major campaign in this direction will have to be launched. it is necessary to recommend to the member states that progressive training be provided for all members of the legal professions, which should be a prerequisite for appointment to higher grades for former members and for admission to the legal professions for new members. the existing programmes grotius and falcone are outdated by the developments. in conclusion, i would like to say a few words about improving the level of application of community law, an area where the committee on petitions has an important role to play. it is the nerve centre of the relations between the european citizen, the european parliament and the commission. the commission's re-evaluation is a prerequisite for revealing weaknesses in the practical application of community law. i support mrs álvarez's proposals, which are in fact the first steps towards the creation of a european administrative law.
- (el) mr president, as one of the two representatives of the european parliament at the intergovernmental conference, i am indebted to the portuguese presidency for its extremely honourable welcome and the truly remarkable conditions of cooperation it has set for us. i hope that the french presidency will do the same. the european union is at the most crucial juncture in its history. the helsinki decision on the great enlargement raises the vital question of the institutional framework within which the european union of some 27 states can function. certainly not in the one currently in force. i fully agree with the relevant remarks made by mr seguro on the many successes of the portuguese presidency. however, in feira, apart from the issue of enhanced cooperation, the igc agenda did not enrich itself; on the contrary, it was mainly concerned with the relics of amsterdam. this position of the european leaders is, i believe, problematic for the following reason: this igc was supposed to meet to examine the institutional conditions under which enlargement can take place. but was not the consolidation of the credibility of the european union one of these institutional conditions? how can this credibility be enhanced without a charter of fundamental rights, without radical legal measures against undemocratic behaviour by states, without a common foreign and defence policy, without constitutionalising the treaties? this leaves the european parliament, if no progress is made until nice, with a huge dilemma as to whether to approve enlargement. i would like to make two points. first, we feel that the power given to us by the treaties to approve or reject enlargement is a historical responsibility, not a means of imposing opinions. secondly, our decision to approve or reject enlargement must, in order to be historically responsible, imply that we believe that institutional changes are sufficient for the european union to welcome 10 or 11 new member states. on the eve of feira, and this is not by chance, of course, an extra-institutional dialogue was launched, with the german and french foreign ministers as protagonists. a very interesting dialogue, brewing new ideas and, on the whole, strengthening the european vision, regardless of whether we agree or disagree on certain points. however, it is a dialogue that reverses the logic of the igc's work today and remains to this day a dialogue exclusively between france and germany. has this extra-institutional dialogue influenced the text of the feira conclusions? is that why the conclusions on institutional questions were so succinct? does feira, in relation to the franco-german dialogue, mean that the igc was already obsolete before it was even completed? i hope not. it is significant that the feira agenda merely added enhanced cooperation. this is being promoted as an urgent and essential issue. in this connection, i would like to make the following remarks: i consider that it is both right and necessary, for the future of the european union, to reflect on the question of the current veto and the risk of blocking it entails, which should be prevented, but i also consider it equally necessary to protect the institutional unity of the european union. mr brok and i have stressed this on several occasions during the igc. i have a question, however: have we made it clear to the applicant countries that the debate on enhanced cooperation is now more specifically about them and that this is why we are rushing to complete the regulations before the new partners join the european union? the central message of the franco-german dialogue is clear: it recognizes the need for two european speeds. the feira summit is historic, not so much for what was said on institutional matters, but, i believe, mainly for what was not said.

improving their infrastructure and by reorienting the former wartime and planned economies towards market conditions. following the decisions of the lisbon european council, which provided that stabilisation and association agreements with the western balkan countries should be preceded by asymmetric trade liberalisation, the commission should shortly present a concrete proposal extending existing trade preferences by abolishing the remaining tariff ceilings for certain industrial products and improving market access for agricultural products. the stability pact for south-eastern europe aims in a general way to help the countries of the region to achieve stability and economic prosperity throughout the region. to achieve this objective, a south-east europe regional table was set up and a working table on economic reconstruction, which includes trade relations. the importance of liberalizing and facilitating trade both between the countries of the region and between the region and the union was underlined by both donors and beneficiaries at the regional conference for the financing of the pact, held in brussels at the end of march this year. in all areas covered by council. - the european union is fully implementing all un security council resolutions, including those concerning iraq. resolution 1284 adopted on 17 december 1999 aims to resume un inspections and at the same time to improve the oil-for-food programme and to suspend sanctions against iraq. the european union calls on the iraqi government to cooperate fully with the un and its subsidiary bodies. the council notes that since the end of 1998 no verification has been carried out to verify that iraq is actually complying with the relevant security council resolutions, in particular resolution 687. the embargo imposed on iraq therefore remains in force. it is true, and we are particularly aware of this, that in iraq the humanitarian situation resulting from the embargo is serious and even worrying, especially for iraqi children. the iraqi government bears the primary responsibility for this situation. however, as the panel of experts chaired by ambassador amorim noted in april 1999, the iraqi people would not have to suffer such deprivations without the continuation of the measures taken in 1991 by the security council. the international community and the iraqi government should do everything possible to ensure that the oil-for-food programme fully benefits the iraqi people. in this regard, the council welcomes the establishment of a un working group to make general recommendations on how to improve the effectiveness of un sanctions, including by better targeting them, and to avoid unnecessary human suffering. the council took note of the fact that the un security council, in its resolution 1302, invited the un secretary-general to appoint independent experts to prepare an in-depth study of the humanitarian situation in iraq.

- (el) mr president, the report of the european central bank which we are discussing here concerns the year 1999, the first year of the bank's operation after the start of the third stage of emu. it is precisely for this reason that the european parliament's critical approach to the actions of the european central bank should have been more rigorous and demanding. the european parliament is the only community institution with the power to exercise, even ex post facto, control over the european central bank and its policy. in this respect, the report of the economic and monetary committee has not met the expectations of european citizens, since it gives its full support to the policy of the european system of central banks and, in many cases, is more royalist than the king by demanding an even more liberal policy. moreover, it is a provocation to maintain that the euro's global position and its weakness against the dollar are due to the delay in implementing the necessary structural changes, to the detriment of the labour market, products and services, to the delay in promoting the conquest of the public sector of the economy, to the dismantling of labour relations and to the upheaval of labour rights, social rights and social security enjoyed by workers. in pursuit of the famous monetary stability, which it considers to be its sole duty, the policy of the european central bank presented clearly contradictory aspects, since, always with the same aim - dynamism in the european economy. on this point, too, he is very likely right. however, his remark raises the question of whether the monetary policy which has been in place for more than a year is the most appropriate one to restore the dynamism which, in the opinion of each of us, our economy needs to regain. unlike the rapporteur, who fully agrees with the european central bank's argument, many of us believe that monetary policy has proved too dogmatic, too conservative and prone to recalling the risk of inflation. all this is forgivable, since conservatism, even some dogmatism, is inherent in the role of central banker. however, the central bank has no right to promote the fight against inflation in the eyes of the public, which is of course an obligation imposed on it by the treaty, when it is itself that gives the definition of inflation at its own discretion. the bank cannot say to us: 'i am the one who stops inflation and inflation is what i say it is, what i judge it to be, even if my judgment is subjective and arbitrary, unrelated to other developments in the economic situation, or even unrelated to support for employment, which is also one of the obligations of the european central bank.' this approach to economic policy borders on the dangerous, especially today. europe's economic recovery has absorbed all the unused productive potential and new investment is needed to sustain the pace of this recovery. is the recent rise in interest rates, under the pretext of inflationary risk, the most appropriate policy to encourage investment? is the 0 to 2% inflation target compatible with the necessary expansion of the investment economy, or should the policy review of the inflation target be considered within the twelve, so that the bank would certainly retain its freedom to pursue an objective, which would, however, be specified to it and placed under the political responsibility of european governments?
- (el) the agreement between the european union and cyprus provides that the cost of participation of cypriot enterprises in the programme will be fully covered by the republic of cyprus. however, in the case of the central european candidate countries, this cost is covered by community resources. although the cost of the agreement is not excessive, the sum in itself is not a problem; the question is about the substance. in addition, cyprus also covers the cost of its participation in other programmes. it is a flagrant injustice to the detriment of the cypriot people. i would also like to point out that the total cost of adjusting cyprus to the acquis communautaire is estimated at between 500 and 900 million cypriot liras, or between eur 1 and 1.8 billion, while the funding granted to the republic of cyprus by the european union is infinitely less. it is clear that the economic relationship between cyprus and the european union is unequal, damaging to the republic of cyprus and that the cypriot people have nothing to gain from membership. for all the above reasons, we, the members of the greek communist party, did not vote in favour, but we abstained.

in writing. -the letter - the legal basis - and even more so the spirit in which electronic commerce is developing and expanding at breakneck speed, do not ensure the protection of personal data concerning the privacy of citizens. on the contrary, they are a cause for deep concern not only because they lead to the mutilation and restructuring of the market and trade itself, but also because citizens are increasingly at risk of having their rights taken away, of being subjected to technology, to the savage laws of the capitalist market, of having their privacy upset and their very existence reduced to a cheap, easily modelled and profit-generating raw material. the application of technological innovations, and even more so those of the spectacular and rapid development of the information society, will bring about profound changes in the social future. while these innovations offer enormous opportunities, they also raise the risk of abuse, which should encourage us and oblige us to be particularly vigilant about the priority of the human factor, respect for the fundamental rights and freedoms of citizens. for our part, there is no question of stopping progress and technology, but there is also no question of allowing them to develop without taking into account man and his needs, in defiance of cultural values and human dignity, attacked on fundamental and inalienable rights, such as the inviolability of privacy and personal data. the proposals and policies for e-commerce, and the measures promoted to support it, reflect a design that is exclusively in the service of profit and competitiveness. the driving force behind this policy is the huge and growing sums of money made available and in circulation to meet the needs of the large multinational companies in this field, so that the obstacles to their rapid adaptation to the sudden changes in such a market can be removed, and so that they can gain huge and rapid profits as well as influence. the proposed social model, which is derived from the texts and proposals of the european union, is strongly supported by unbridled competition and the excessive increase in hyper-profit, as much as it supports them. this conception does not constitute a guarantee, but, on the contrary, an intolerable threat to the future of society and of man: uncertainty, insecurity, rupture with his own needs, categorization of citizens and workers who will no longer shape their environment, but will be shaped by it, so that they put themselves, in the most efficient, fastest and cheapest way, at the service of those who determine this environment. the term 'relevant protection' used in the report is, for us, a constant condition and a change in the concept of personal data protection, when, as a deterrent and safety valve, sanctions and compensation are announced for the transgression of doors that have been wide open. the personal data of eu citizens are already circulating in third countries with virtually no controls, without us even being aware of the extent of the arbitrary acts already committed. while the electronic surveillance system echelon allows, at a distance, the intolerable violation of any democratic control, allows american multinationals to monitor and spy on a large scale to the detriment of european companies, of all the economic, social, political and cultural activities of the member states and of the private life of the citizens of the union, it is hypocritical to approach this huge issue gently, by indulging in reassuring words. the existing protection framework, as well as the proposed standardised and conventional clauses, are too inadequate, lacking in detail and ineffective. it cannot be conceived nor can there be any appeal to good intentions in a climate of abhorrent competition and the absolute sovereignty of profit. it is only by euphemism that we would dare to call a "safe harbour" the heart of unlimited monopolies whose accelerated formation, expansion and free action is facilitated by the frank and aggressive imperialism of the internet.
the rapporteur. - (el) mr president, the regulation under consideration is part of a series of measures designed to remedy the situation created by the mad cow crisis, to develop a prevention policy to avoid such situations and to strengthen consumer confidence in beef. to this end, the regulation establishes a system for the identification of animals by all kinds of technical means. the aim of this system is traceability, i.e. the possibility of linking each portion of meat ultimately purchased by the consumer to the animal from which it originates. it is already a highly technical and heavy system. it also provides for a system of marking meat and meat products. the consumer will have, thanks to the label that will be affixed to the product he buys, the rudimentary, but useful and indispensable information about the meat he buys. mr president, this system has been in place for four years. it is true that we have been very late and we have often denounced it. you will recall all the disputes we had with the other union bodies last december, when we suspected that once again the application of the regulation was being postponed and delayed. today we are in a situation which i want to explain in a simplified way. after the first reading in the european parliament, the council approved a common position. the common position contains the largest number of amendments ever tabled by the european parliament. the text of the regulation is simplified. it is easy to apply. the control system is strengthened. however, the common position does not accept our amendment which abolished the mention of the category of animals from which the meat is derived from the mandatory labelling of meat. we had abolished this indication in order to simplify the labelling so that the consumer could be informed with greater clarity and to reduce the cost to meat traders. the argument put forward in support of maintaining the mention of the category of animals is that this indication corresponds to the needs, habits and preferences of consumers, at least in certain countries of the union. the question is therefore whether we will persist in asking for the text of the regulation to be amended, which will most likely lead to a conciliation process, delaying the application of the regulation, whereas, on the other hand, the european parliament, i would remind you, has spoken in favour of the application of the regulation as soon as possible, having even accused and continuing to accuse the other bodies of the european union of their unacceptable delay. and i am referring to the amendment on the category of animals, not to a second amendment on minced meat, because i do not think that the latter meets any particular need. cut meat, by virtue of the rules laid down in the regulation, shall in any event be inseparable from the animal from which it was obtained; the other questions are technical questions of production and we would do well not to change what the rules say. i now turn to the environment committee, for which i have the honour of being rapporteur. the committee on the environment decided, by a satisfactory majority, to insist on the amendment of the text. it approved the fundamental amendment concerning the abolition of the mention of the category 'animals'. unfortunately, your rapporteur was not convinced of the need, the usefulness and the purpose of an amendment to the text and felt obliged, to his great regret, to take the view that was contrary to ours, namely the rejection of any amendment and the acceptance of the common position. i hope that the wisdom of this house will provide the best and most reliable solution for the rapid application of the regulation we are debating and will resolve, i hope, this temporary dispute between the rapporteur and his committee.
the rapporteur. - (el) mr president, i agree with everything the chair of our committee said. i have outlined all the discussion and controversy that has arisen from the regulation. in the last two lines i have expressed my personal opinion, to my great regret, in order not to speak again as a speaker. i would like to assure the president that i am a firm adherent of the procedures, perhaps more so than many other colleagues, both in this and in other matters.

the commission has not yet received any information on the implementation of the waste management directive, and it is therefore not possible to conclude that the commission has received any information on the implementation of the waste management directive. the commission has very little information on waste management in greece. the country did not report on the application of legislation in this area between 1989 and 1997. however, at the end of last year greece replied to four questionnaires concerning four waste directives. the responses obtained show that 280 000 tonnes of hazardous waste were produced in the country - no year is given - of which 95 760 tonnes were not recycled, while greece indicates that it has no hazardous waste treatment or disposal infrastructure. it also states that such waste is either stored temporarily or exported abroad for disposal. the commission considers that greece does not comply with a number of community requirements. as such, it brought proceedings under article 226. the most serious aspects concern the absence of management plans, as required by article 7 of directive 75/442 on waste, article 6 of directive 91/689 on hazardous waste and article 14 of directive 94/62 on packaging and packaging waste; the non-transposition of directive 94/62 and the non-transmission of the information required under article 12 concerning the packaging waste industry; the absence of a programme in accordance with article 6 of directive 91/157 on batteries and accumulators containing dangerous substances; the commission has not yet received any information on the extent of the infringement. directive 94/67 on the incineration of hazardous waste requires a first report for the period 1998-2000 to be submitted by the end of september 2001.

procedural rights but also rights relating to content, which will legitimately protect their interests and will turn into a right to compensation in the event of damage to interests. the obvious necessary reforms of the functioning of the community institutions. i wish the ombudsman good courage in the pursuit of his task.

in writing. -the importance of forests for maintaining the basic ecological balances of the ecosystem of life itself is enormous. unfortunately, the protection and attention they receive is not commensurate with this importance. the enormous disasters that have hit forests in recent years, together with the weakness of the responses to these disasters, are seriously endangering the ecological balance as well as the health, safety and many of the cultural and social values that are inextricably linked to the forest heritage. in recent years, forest areas, particularly those in the mediterranean basin, have been severely affected by deforestation, fires, desertification, abandonment, over-exploitation and insufficient reforestation, resulting in the loss of tens of thousands of valuable hectares of forested land. i will explain to you the dramatic problem of greece, which is the first country in all of europe in terms of the area burned by fire. in greece, only 10% of the burned areas are reforested. the remaining 90% is either abandoned or, for the most part, subject to illegal construction and violation, in the service of economic interests (e.g. tourist enterprises, which are usually always on the side of the ruling government) that benefit from the fires - or perhaps are responsible for them - the insoluble problem of ownership, the lack of forest registers and the state forest inventory, in order to increase their profit. it is a fact that there is a great variety of community forests (northern, temperate, mediterranean and tropical) and, in connection with this, a great variety of problems and peculiarities. it is not enough that the eu forestry strategy, which has been pursued to date, has failed to address these problems, due to its fragmented, cyclical, inconsistent and inconsistent policies and insufficient funds. the commission is now proposing to reduce even further the already minimal funds for protecting forests against fires and air pollution. the reason given to justify these proposed cuts is the insufficient absorption of funds in recent years! the problems, however, have not been solved. on the contrary, they have worsened. such a policy is not only hypocritical. she's an accomplice and an arsonist. we, on the other hand, persevere. we also call on the european parliament to remember its repeated resolutions and its positions in favour of strengthened measures to prevent and combat fires and to protect and enhance the forest heritage in all regions of the union. we believe that the community financial resources allocated to the protection of forests were and remain completely insufficient and we call for their increase to be commensurate with the real needs and importance of forests. it is essential, in collaboration with the national and local authorities and the competent bodies, that appropriate initiatives be taken to define an effective forestry policy and to develop immediate and coordinated action which will enable the regeneration and reforestation of devastated forest areas, in accordance with the bioclimatic and ecological conditions of each region and on the basis of current scientific data and methods. we repeat that the european union and the governments must provide the funds necessary to create the necessary infrastructure and to hire staff, which, in greece's view, is totally inadequate. in this way we will contribute to the protection, development, ecological preservation and productive development of the forests of these countries.

in writing. - (el) the objective of this report is to justify the liberalisation of the energy sector, mainly electricity but also natural gas. in essence, it takes away from the state this strategically important productive sector and hands over all its activity to private capital, so that it can multiply its gains. the state is reduced to the role of regulator of monopoly interests and supervisor of the directions that the eu decides to the detriment of the country's economy and the people's interest. the reasoning behind the whole report takes no account of the interests of the people, of the ordinary citizen, or even of the development of each country, but of the interests of the big monopolies. the report, in an effort to embellish the liberalisation of the energy sector, contains a number of inaccuracies. for example, it is not true that in britain competition has helped to reduce prices and increase employment. in the four or five years following the privatisation of electricity, there was, on the contrary, a sharp rise in prices, a reduction in staff, the abandonment of the trajectory of a long-term national programme for the development of electricity. elsewhere, the report itself acknowledges job losses as a result of liberalisation, but estimates that there will be an increase in jobs in non-productive sectors, stock exchange, advertising, brokerage firms, etc. according to a study carried out by the public electricity company in greece, the liberalisation of electricity will reduce its staff from 32 000 to 20 000 people, thus abolishing a large number of services that are useful both to the public electricity company and to the economy of the country, such as the promotion of new technologies, studies of power stations, etc. privatization and liberalization will lead to higher prices, particularly for consumers in the smaller aegean islands, who currently pay less than the cost of the kilowatt. it is equally unacceptable to require a public utility to make available to the private sector an entire transport and distribution network of enormous value in the name of so-called competition. if the eu wanted to have healthy competition, in the sense that it gives to the term, it should rather force individuals to create their own networks and production units in order to compete with public service undertakings, and not force the latter to "gift" their network to their competitors. simply, in this way, they want to facilitate the transfer of wealth to big capital. for exactly the same reason, the separation of the branches is undertaken so that any public service undertaking cannot operate in such a way as to obtain the best result from its vertical activities and, not as a consequence, not be competitive. energy is not just a consumer product. the energy sector is particularly important for the economic development of a country and vital for its defence. therefore, the liberalisation of energy markets not only jeopardises a country's security but also its development. this danger is clear, as the report emphasises, 'public service companies must operate on the european market, in the europe without frontiers. these companies are no longer strictly national, nor are the shareholders". the commission is also proposing to extend the scope of the directive to include the use of the term 'competent authority' to cover all the activities of the european union.
in writing. -(el) once again we are faced with an avalanche of good intentions which, even when they lead to the drafting of positive proposals, are doomed to remain dead letter, since they do not grasp the essence of the problem or even touch the main reason that creates it. the european parliament's proposal contains many positive elements which, however, are cancelled out by its own weakness in tackling the problem of negative developments in the context of work and industrial relations in a global way. the fact that the fundamental problem of the restructuring of industrial relations, the reduction and abolition of workers' fundamental rights and rights acquired is not even raised, and that the problem of working conditions - flexibility of working time and conditions, temporary and parttime work, reduction of social security and protection - is also ignored, the framework in which the proposal is conceived is totally negative and suspensive for the application of any policy in favour of the protection of women workers, before and after pregnancy. we would like to stress the dangers of the strict prohibition of dismissals, particularly at a time when atypical work contracts are widespread, the indirect barriers to the recruitment of pregnant women, the need not only to protect and guarantee maternity leave but also to extend it, the need to define maternity allowances more clearly, the need to maintain stricter and more consistent controls on the night work of pregnant and child-bearing workers. on the one hand, it is essential to strengthen the legal framework necessary to protect the safety and health of pregnant women, by filling its gaps and clarifying its obscurities - "trojan horses" that increase its alteration and violation. on the other hand, there is a lack of political will to ensure that the directive is effectively applied by the governments of the member states, which can be easily demonstrated by examining the position of the member states' control mechanisms in the preceding period. in this respect, the example of greece is characteristic, considering the absence of controls on the obligation of the employer to prepare a detailed assessment of occupational risks at the workplace, which must also include the precise analysis of risks for pregnant and childbearing workers. the reality is that when employability and adaptability are the guiding principles of community employment priorities, protection is non-existent and, in practice, the specific regulations are annulled. the problem is what directions are given, what kind of employment policy is pursued. and unfortunately, the eu - as it confirms to us - persists in drastically limiting benefits and social security, amending at the worst the provisions concerning dismissals, reducing or abolishing protective measures in the context of working conditions. there can be no further illusion about the nature of eu employment policy and social policy in general, or about the interests it serves. that is why, even if we share the positive position of the european parliament on the question of the protection of pregnant workers, we believe that once again this is only a statement of ideas and good intentions. we therefore call on workers to continue their social struggles to achieve a change in the whole of labour policy and to promote a new policy focused on man, on respect for his needs and dignity. the main objective of these struggles is to achieve the principle of full employment and employment stability, and to protect the rights acquired and recognised for all. only in this context can the rights of this particularly sensitive category of pregnant women be guaranteed, the rights and respect for life which is not a raw material for profit and which does not respond to the laws of cost, competition and the ruthless market.

- madam president, although there have been important scientific steps in the fight against aids, the epidemic continues to hit the poor countries more and more, making it increasingly difficult to deal with its many and serious consequences. it is true that preventive and therapeutic interventions in recent years have led to significant improvements in patient survival in developed countries, but the same is not true for poor countries. the spread of the disease has exceeded any ominous prediction, especially in african countries, and as reported, five people die every minute from the disease, which is becoming a public health threat. every day in these countries, babies are born infected with the virus because of a complete lack of appropriate programmes for expectant mothers. also, the cost of treatment is so high that few patients are receiving this basic right. in this context, i consider the proposal to reduce the price of medicines for people affected by aids in these areas to be very important and i would urge the commission and the council to support this effort by putting pressure on the pharmaceutical companies. also, coordinated efforts should be made to develop comprehensive educational, preventive, curative and welfare programmes in developing countries, at least until the safe use of the vaccine is achieved, which we all want. until then, prevention and treatment are the most important factors in controlling the spread of the disease, and these are benefits that all citizens should receive regardless of where they live.

counsel. - allow me, mr president, to answer the questions put by mr theonas and mrs kinnock jointly, because they both concern the need to achieve the abolition of child labour in the long term. as regards the question put by mr theonas, it should be stressed that the european union attaches great importance to the abolition of the exploitation of children, which deprives them of their rights to a normal childhood, to education and to freedom from forced labour. the european union actively supports the action of the united nations, the international labour organisation and the united nations children's fund, better known as unicef, in this field and welcomes the adoption of the ilo convention on the prohibition and immediate elimination of the worst forms of child labour, ratified by the united states on 2 december 1999, and will ensure that this convention effectively contributes to the elimination of the most intolerable forms of child labour. the european union regrets that the united states has not yet ratified the united nations convention on the rights of the child. in bilateral contacts our representatives have invited the united states to proceed with this ratification. the convention, ratified by the member states, is of paramount importance to the european union, which is very actively involved in the current process of preparing the special session of the united nations general assembly on children, to be held in september 2001. the conference will take stock of progress in the field of children's rights over the past ten years. in its political dialogue with the united states, the european union often raises human rights issues, and the council takes note of the case raised by the honourable member. as regards the question raised by mrs kinnock, the council fully shares the view that there is a need to ensure proper preparation for the special session of the united nations general assembly devoted to the follow-up to the world summit for children, which will take place in september 2001. the european union has begun to prepare actively for this event. it has included it among its priorities for the 55th session of the un general assembly. although the success of this event depends to a large extent on the commitment of the international community as a whole, the council is aware that the european union could and should play a key role in this preparatory process, a role which was encouraged in informal contacts with unicef representatives. in addition, the council pays constant attention to the rights of the child in all fora where human rights issues are discussed. it has placed the preparation of the special session on the agenda of its human rights group and is planning to devote a specific section of the annual report of the european union on human rights to this subject. finally, the french presidency has proposed to its partners the organization of a european day of the rights of the child, which could be held - i say this with reservations, because it has not yet been officially decided - on 20 november 2000. the council encourages the signing of the two optional protocols to the convention on the rights of the child, which the united nations general assembly adopted and opened for signature on 25 may 2000, and we hope that many countries will sign them at the millennium summit which opens tomorrow and will implement these two optional protocols.
- mr president, i should like to thank mr moscovici for the quality and substance of his reply and for the fact that the european union considers it necessary to ratify the international conventions of the international labour organisation and the united nations convention on the rights of the child. i would just like to point out, as a supplementary question, that ratification is very important, but often it is not enough. because if i am not mistaken, the united states has ratified the new international labour organization convention on the rights of the child, but it is not being applied. and where i think the minister needs to tell us more is whether the european union, whether the council will demand from the united states, in the context of bilateral contacts, the implementation of the provisions of the international labour organization convention and the united nations convention on the rights of the child.
the council has always attached great importance to the social dialogue. however, it is not for the commission, and it is understandable why, to advocate any model of social dialogue at national level, as this is entirely the responsibility of each member state. however, as far as the social dialogue at european level is concerned, the european social partners are already heavily involved in two dimensions of community action, firstly the coordination of economic policies, and secondly the further implementation of the european employment strategy. in the latter area, the social partners participate in the work of the standing committee on employment, together with the council and the commission, with a view to facilitating the coordination of the employment policies of the member states by harmonizing them with community objectives. our social partners are also involved in the work of the employment committee, the consultation of the social partners in this context being, this time, of an informal nature. in the context of efforts to strengthen the european employment strategy, the social partners, including at national level, will be invited to play a greater role in defining, implementing and evaluating the guidelines within their remit when the employment guidelines are revised in 2001. particular emphasis will be placed on modernising the organisation of work, on lifelong learning and on increasing the employment rate, especially for women. it should also be noted that the commission is required, in accordance with article 138 of the treaty, to consult the social partners on the possible direction of community action before submitting proposals in the field of social policy. for example, the commission recently consulted the social partners on the possible direction of community action on the modernisation and improvement of industrial relations. as regards the modernisation of the european social model, the social partners will be involved in the work of the new social protection committee, in the same way as they were already involved in the work of the former employment and labour market committee and its successor, the employment committee. the fifteen will have to adopt a european social agenda at the nice european council in december 2000, in accordance with the conclusions of the lisbon european council. this is a major priority for the french presidency. a commission communication adopted by the college on 28 june serves as a working document for this agenda. it will be the subject of contributions and consultations under the french presidency, and it is of course understandable that the social partners will be closely involved in the elaboration of this social agenda.

counsel. - it is true that the extension of qualified majority voting to areas of activity of the european union which now require unanimity is precisely one of the major issues of the intergovernmental conference currently under way. this is a vital democratic issue. in a democracy, decisions are made by majority vote, not unanimity. in this context, article 151 is one of the provisions which may be affected by the qualified majority rule. mrs zorba will agree with me that it is still too early to say what the future of the work of the intergovernmental conference will be, although it seems to me possible, i say this with caution, that a consensus will be reached quickly on article 151, because there can be no disagreement on everything. as regards the way in which article 151 (4) of the treaty has been applied, i will simply reply that respect for and promotion of the diversity of european cultures are at the heart of the whole community edifice.

- mr president, i fully share mr morillon's suggestion that the hypocrisy with which certain problems are sometimes dealt with for political or other reasons should be abandoned. i think there is no doubt that the issues covered by the three amendments which the council rejected are real, as the council's position in this case may suggest, at least, a hypocritical attempt to dismiss them or to downplay their importance. however, these amendments, which reflect the european parliament's consistent views, are all three of substantial weight and the rapporteur rightly insists on their adoption, since they guarantee protection against the risks of the turkish authorities constructing a nuclear power station in earthquake-prone areas, explicitly mention the death penalty and clearly state turkey's obligation to recognise and protect the cultural identity of minorities, in the context of the more general obligation to respect the rule of law and human rights, and to promote the undoubted kurdish problem and the need to solve it, not with turkish bombings against civilians in the region, which took place on 18 august in cologne. i hope that the insistence of our parliament on adopting these specific points will also help the turkish side to realise, even if - as mr morillon rightly observed - it seems rather reluctant to realise that the path towards europe is not a one-way street of goodwill and tolerant understanding on the part of the member states of the european union in the face of turkey's alleged peculiarities, but requires a corresponding goodwill on the part of the turkish authorities and turkish society to conform to the rules of law and morality governing the european union, which apply to all the candidate countries, rules which, unfortunately, are still being ignored by turkey, not only in the turkish republican problem and greek-cypriot relations, but also in many other cases. a practical and comprehensive change in this turkish attitude and mentality will give some encouragement to those who, with a spirit of rather over-optimism, wanted to speed up turkey's european path and will help to shorten the long and difficult road - i am referring again to mr morillon - of turkey's accession to the european family.
the european parliament has already made a number of proposals to the council, and i am sure that the council will be able to accept them. we warned then that this decision would not only not help, as many had claimed, towards democratization, the improvement of the living conditions of the peoples of turkey, but would encourage the regime to further intensify its oppression. nine months later, we are sad to see that we have been confirmed, because it is true that we would have preferred to have been denied. the council is today proposing to release eur 135 million for the economic and social development of turkey. we are very much afraid that a considerable part of it will be used, despite the controls that can be exercised, to strengthen the repressive mechanism. let me give you some clues. as we all know, despite the promises made from time to time, the constitution remains deeply undemocratic; the criminal code itself; turkey's prisons currently hold over 15,000 political prisoners in inhumane conditions, which are being upgraded with the intensification of solitary confinement, which is causing huge reactions in turkey, including from the istanbul bar association, which in a special report denounces these measures; we have recent arrests on the occasion of events for the 1st of september, the world day of peace; cyprus shall continue to be occupied; the european parliament has been very active in this area, and i am sure that the european parliament will be very active in this area. and i also want to say that gem says the same thing about greek-turkish relations. the commission has already made a number of proposals to the council, and i am sure that the council will be able to accept them. so you want to use this money as bait to get even further into the turkish economy, to plunder even more of the wealth of the turkish people. we are not going to say give it or don't give it, we just want to note under what conditions and for what reason this money is given.

non-settlers - half of whom are still in cyprus, saying: 'finally, turks, let us join the european union, since you want to join too, let us join earlier.' in this sense, because in this connection europe's political character, its prospects, its principles are at stake, i am sure that commissioner verheugen will assure us that the commission, as guardian of these principles, will commit the accession process of turkey to these principles. and i think that's the main issue, this routing and this course. i do not think, mr verheugen, that all of us can accept on the one hand voting for parliaments, voting for governments, and on the other hand still having a military establishment that determines the political conditions on behalf of all of us. the commission's proposal is a very good one. we, as greeks, want turkey in the european union, we want a democratic turkey, because this democratic turkey will be the starting point for the absence of crises in the wider region, crises that we have experienced for many years and that we have all experienced negatively.
- mr president, commissioner, ladies and gentlemen, the audiovisual media, by their very appearance, have been a powerful factor in ideological intervention. in the digital age, controlling them becomes strategically important for shaping consciences and lifestyles, since whoever controls them will also determine what kind of information they channel. for the communist party of greece, control must belong to the public. not because we have illusions that under capitalism governments will allow for full and meaningful information, but because then we can have the maximum possible social control. the commission, on the contrary, gives the public authorities the role of the poor relative and the market laws the role of the essential regulator. the apparent anti-monopoly policy aims at creating oligopolies which will damage the objectivity of information, but will ensure profits and ideological manipulation, by controlling the flow of information by some european monopolies, to the detriment of any notion of freedom and democracy. the problem for us, ladies and gentlemen, is not whether information is controlled by american or european monopolies. the real problem is for the people to develop their resistance and to assert their right to meaningful information.

in writing. - (el) the european parliament should insist on the three amendments: - on the exclusion of nuclear energy from the earthquake-prone areas of turkey, as regards the production system; - on the protection of cultural identity and the abolition of the death penalty; - on the contribution to the solution of the kurdish problem. that is why i voted in favour of these amendments. but i think that the european parliament should have stuck to its position on the first reading amendments, article 5 (turkey's accession criteria), article 15 (suspension of cooperation with turkey in the event of obstacles on the issues of democracy, the rule of law, human rights, protection of minorities), article 35 (annual report on respect for democratic principles, the rule of law, human rights and fundamental freedoms, and respect for international law). unfortunately, the european parliament's committee on foreign affairs has succumbed to the pressure of the council, which is favouring a realpolitik without principles with the turkish regime, thus depriving the very citizens of that country of the democratic impetus that the eu could give.
in writing. - (el) the eu's policy towards the mediterranean countries is aimed not at developing these countries but at increasing the influence of european capital. this is the objective of the meda funding programme. the creation of a euro-mediterranean free trade area is intended to facilitate the penetration of european industrial products into the markets of these countries and at the same time to subject all their wealth to a system of plunder. the eu's appeal to the consolidation of democracy, the rule of law and human rights is just the usual pretext for interfering in the internal affairs of other countries. the structural reforms which are being strengthened through meda are mainly aimed at imposing on these countries the famous laws of the market, the domination of those who are economically stronger. the result will be to push these countries even further into underdevelopment and make them satellites of the eu, due to unequal partnership agreements. the communist party of greece is a strong supporter of cooperation between peoples on an equal footing and without interference by one country in the internal affairs of another. it stresses in particular the responsibility of the developed capitalist countries for the present situation of the underdeveloped countries, which is the result of the plunder carried out hitherto at their expense. this is one more reason why developed countries have an obligation to help underdeveloped countries to expand their production base and raise the living standards of their people. since the previous meda programme and the amendments proposed are far from the objectives mentioned above, we, the gcp meps, vote against the report by mr valdivielso de cué.
in writing. - (el) we vote against the joint resolution on the priorities for the eu's external action, because we believe that it goes even further, and moreover on the proposal of the ep, to deprive the member states of any possibility of conducting an independent foreign and defence policy and to integrate them into the emerging common foreign and security policy, which has already been marked by political and military interventions in the internal affairs of third countries in order to serve the imperialist interests of the eu, in defiance of any notion of international law and at the expense of the peoples of the eu and other countries. this is what has recently happened in the case of kosovo and more generally in yugoslavia. the militarisation of the eu, the pursuit of armaments, the harmonisation of military equipment and the arms industries, the imposition of the will of the strongest in the jungle of 'globalisation' and the market economy, are in contradiction with the aspirations and struggles of peoples for peace, the political and peaceful settlement of disputes, the primacy of international law. it is clear that this policy is in line with the recent council decisions on the prohibition of all access to documents concerning defence and security policies. the overall approach is to keep the people of the member states out of the decision-making centres and to prevent them from influencing developments; in other words, to erect barriers and obstacles so that the popular movement, the national parliaments and the european parliament cannot exercise any democratic control. we are deeply concerned that the eu is now, in fact, violating even its own treaties, and is trying, by indirect means, to evade its internal conflicts. it is seeking, at an even faster pace, to defend the interests of european big capital, to conquer new markets, and even to support the euro militarily. the new order puts forward new demands on the less developed peoples and countries in order to establish its dominance. in practice, we are favouring a policy that faithfully serves big business and that aligns itself with the united states, that obeys its orders and promotes the new order at the expense of the people. this helps to explain why the eu is not bothered by the barbaric invasion of cyprus and the continued occupation of 38% of its territory, nor by turkey's bombing of iraq. the question, in our view, is not just whether or not the common actions will be financed from the community budget and under what conditions, but to put an end to this policy, which is disastrous for the interests of the peoples and for peace. the question is not whether mr solana or each of the pesc members will make annual statements to the european parliament, but how the peoples of europe will take their destinies into their own hands and be able to intervene decisively to impose peace, fraternity and international cooperation on the basis of mutual interest. the joint resolution confines itself to the two aforementioned issues of secondary importance, and comes to the arbitrary and at the same time particularly dangerous conclusion that the 'intergovernmental system' which is the basis of the eu's common foreign and defence policy is the cause of its failure, and that its success depends on its communitization. it is thus disorienting the peoples and leaving them at the mercy of all the choices of big capital which are now out of control. for all the above reasons, we believe that the 'common' resolution in question demonstrates in the most dramatic way how urgent it is for the peoples to organise their common resistance and counter-offensive against this criminal policy, to take the initiative of the operations.

in writing. - (el) there are so many problems and obstacles to the rights of all european citizens moving within the eu or residing in a country other than their own, and also of third-country nationals legally established in the eu, that for many of them the famous right to 'free movement' becomes nonexistent or resembles a continuous and formidable obstacle race. students, pensioners and certain categories of workers, particularly those in atypical jobs and part-time jobs, face a whole range of difficulties and problems in staying in the host country because of the multiplicity and volume of formalities for the granting and renewal of a residence permit and other difficulties in proving that they have sufficient resources. in reality, the famous 'european area of freedom' does not exist or is subject to such oppressive economic criteria that the right of residence, although recognised as an 'inalienable' right, is granted only to financially independent individuals. in other words, we are dealing with a complete distortion of ideas and a gross misrepresentation of the lives of hundreds of thousands of europeans and their families. if we add to all this the unacceptable phenomena of misinterpretation of the notion of public security and public order, not only is freedom of movement and establishment undermined and trampled underfoot in many cases, but it also serves as a springboard for the violation of fundamental democratic and individual rights, such as the protection of personal data. under the pretext of public policy, the schengen information system stores, transmits and processes data on citizens in breach of the treaties, community and international law. we also draw attention to the numerous cases of evictions of citizens for economic or general prevention reasons which in no way constitute real burdens and sufficiently serious to justify removal by force. for reasons of safeguarding basic fundamental rights, but also the very dignity of european citizens and third-country nationals legally resident in the eu, it is necessary to simplify the procedures for establishment and residence without delay, in order to organise and ensure the unhindered exercise of this right and to eliminate inequalities and violations of fundamental democratic rights. member states will have to simplify and make as flexible as possible the procedures and formalities required for students and pensioners with regard to a sufficient level of resources, the granting and renewal of residence permits. the issue and renewal of residence permits for migrant workers should be facilitated, and the system of social protection and medical care should be improved in order to put an end to discrimination against these workers. family reunification should also be facilitated by simplifying the criteria for the settlement of family members; the system for granting and surrendering residence permits for a period of at least five years should be simplified in general, if the applicant meets the required conditions. finally, the misuse of public policy grounds, which makes european citizens 'ordinary suspects' for reasons unrelated to public security, in flagrant violation of the rule of law, the declaration of human rights and the charter of fundamental rights, must be stopped.
commission. – water will be a crucial issue for the middle east in the coming decades. the region has the lowest per capita availability of water anywhere in the world and it is steadily decreasing. water is indeed one of the main issues of the middle east peace process, not only in the negotiations between israel and the palestinians but also between israel and syria. the commission's policy focuses on the various dimensions of the water question in the middle east, the security as well as the economic, environmental and social dimensions. just and comprehensive water agreements between israel and its neighbours, sustained by effective regional cooperation, are necessary for lasting peace in the region. the commission is an active participant in, and a main donor to, the multilateral water working group of the mepp. as a concrete contribution we have committed another eur 4 million in 1999 for the completion of the regional water data banks. we also promote the concept of a regional cooperation structure. the council's special taskforce on water in the middle east has met with the israeli, jordanian and the palestinian water commissioners and is actively coordinating eu water policy. the commission's policy aims at helping the region to achieve a sustainable management of the scarce water resources in combination with assistance to explore new resources. current water management and consumption in the middle east cannot be sustained. estimates suggest that in the overall mediterranean region agriculture absorbs over two thirds of total national water consumption, while accounting for only one third of gnp and labour. this discrepancy is even sharper in the middle east. therefore assisting reform of domestic water policies in the countries of the middle east is a priority. indeed the euro-mediterranean foreign ministers conference in stuttgart in 1999 decided to make water a priority for the partnership. as a first result the eur 40 million action plan adopted by the euro-mediterranean ministerial conference on local water management in turin in october 1999 would provide a further opportunity for cooperation in this sector. a call for proposals will be published soon.
- thank you for your answer, commissioner. the question i put was not only to highlight the seriousness of the problem and its impact on the middle east peace process but also to make the lack of water a serious political problem. everyone is talking about the impending water crises. something has to be done. i would ask the commissioner what initiatives will be taken to bring about international law rules in the management of water resources, so as to eliminate any hotbeds of local or regional political competition, precursors of crises and a specific type of imperialism. the same answer that refers to the israel-syria issue leads a little further, to the tigris and euphrates, to turkey, syria and elsewhere. i believe that international rules on the management of water resources should be established, containing clear conditions for all the countries concerned through which rivers cross more than one country. the same should apply to lakes surrounded by more than one country.

the rapporteur. mr president, the proposal for a directive we are discussing today concerns the restriction of the marketing and use of azo dyes used in textile and leather dyes. the reason for these restrictions is the reasonable suspicion that these substances are carcinogenic. the annex to the directive contains a list of textile and leather products in which the use of these suspect substances is prohibited. many countries have already introduced such prohibitions into their legislation and most companies established in the european union fall into this category. the commission has already taken a number of measures to ensure that the internal market is not a one-sided market. finally, the directive naturally also contains a method of analysis for the detection of nitrogenous colouring matters whose use is prohibited. the committee on the environment, public health and consumer protection has accepted the principle on which this directive is based. the amendments adopted unanimously are therefore improving. i shall mention only two of them. the committee on the environment is of the opinion that the list of products in which the use of azo dyes will be prohibited, which is contained in the annex to the directive, should not be indicative. it must be closed, complete, explicit and nominal. the commission has already taken a number of measures to ensure that the directive is not applied in a way that would be contrary to the principle of the single market, which is the very purpose of the directive. that is the essence of amendment no 3. the second case i would like to mention is amendment no 2, which introduces recital 7a, which excludes handmade oriental carpets from this directive. the exemption was based on the fact that, due to the hand-made manufacture of these carpets, sampling is de facto excluded. the commission has already taken the necessary steps to ensure that the quality of the carpets is not affected by the quality of the products. however, it is recommended that importers develop a quality label. amendment no 6 tabled by mrs roth-behrendt and mrs müller within the deadline restates recital 7a and proposes an expiry date for the exclusion of oriental carpets from the directive. and as such, 1 january 2006 is proposed. i think that is a good proposal and i would suggest that it be adopted. finally, mrs ferrer tabled amendment no 7, also on time, which proposes adding one more to the list of 21 azo dyes in the annex, in addition to the two substances provided for in amendment no 4. the substance proposed by mrs ferrer is already in the annex. the commission has already made a number of proposals to the council, and i am sure that the council will be able to accept them.

commission. - mr president, i would like to thank mr piecyk for his work. the question of inland ports and intermodal terminals is a very complex one and i would like to stress the broad consensus that has been reached. we all agree that the transeuropean transport network must be a multimodal network. we all agree that it should include the main connection points, and i think we also agree that the current provisions of the transeuropean networks on ports are not satisfactory and that the strengthening of the role of ports in the network will also serve to consolidate the transeuropean networks. parliament and the commission share - i believe - a broad agreement on most areas of debate. we agree that the inclusion of too many ports in the network may affect this concept of transeuropean and that therefore ports of greater strategic importance should be included. i also think that parliament and the commission agree that inland terminals are an important part of the network and play a fundamental role in this whole process. we are not talking about financial aid today, but i would like to say at this point that we believe that this financial support should be used properly and not be used for superstructure projects which may, in the end, have a negative impact on competition. however, there are some points where it will be difficult to make progress, because although we can make more progress between parliament and the commission, we must not forget the fundamental role of the council in this decision-making process. i will make a few comments on the fundamental amendments. amendment no 4: increase the threshold for inland ports from 300 to 500 thousand tonnes. it is a proposal which is perfectly acceptable to the commission. the piecyk report introduces the justification precisely for this idea so that it is not an excessive number of installations that, in the end, have the treatment of transeuropean networks. the commission considers that it would be ideal to include only those ports which, in addition to having these limits, have a capacity for transhipment facilities. we think that this is not a view accepted by the council, but we do not think that quantity alone is sufficient. we believe that a quantitative and a qualitative element must be introduced. sea ports: amendments nos 5, 9 and 10. we too could accept the idea of raising the threshold to one and a half million tonnes or two hundred thousand passengers and we could also agree to the abolition of the categorical classifications carried out by the council. this is a point which will no doubt be the subject of further discussion in the council. amendments nos 6 and 7 reintroduce the inland terminal map and are acceptable to the commission. although we consider that the most important problem is to emphasize that inland terminals are an integral part of the network, and we do not consider the problem of whether they appear on a map or not to be relevant. that is a problem - in our view - of secondary importance. the important thing is that they are definitely part of the network. we think that perhaps the intermediate solution could be to abandon the intermodal terminal map and to draft guidelines in a more precise, more forceful way if you will, emphasising that intermodal terminals are part of the network, and not to enter into a debate on the map which would make things more difficult. the problem of superstructures being excluded from community financing in amendments nos 8 and 10. the commission can also support these amendments, but we do think that this is a difficult debate. we support the aim of the amendments but, nevertheless, we cannot support their wording. firstly, because we think it is inappropriate to talk about funding in these guidelines and secondly because we do not think it is necessary to make this reference. the fact is that the types of projects to which parliament refers when it speaks of superstructures are not included in projects of common interest and therefore do not receive funding. it is therefore a somewhat unnecessary debate. a comment on amendment no 15 by mr von wogau. amendment no 15 is acceptable to the commission as an objective. we think that the idea is correct, although we think that here too, as regards the wording, we should analyse it with a little more care to avoid problems. finally, as regards community expenditure and public aid and the question of powers, we think that this is also important and that it would be appropriate to discuss it in the future and, in this connection, we believe that we should work towards a solution to this and the other problems, taking into account the interests of both parties, and we believe that agreement is possible on the basis of the excellent work done by parliament.
the rapporteur. - mr president, commissioner, this is a highly technical issue, but it has aspects which concern the daily life of the european citizen and the single market itself. as things stand today, the legal limits for the maximum length of passenger vehicles vary widely across the member states of the european union. in seven countries the limit is 40 feet [12 m], in one country it is 50 feet [13 m], in two others it is 50 feet [14 m] and in five others it is 50 feet [15 m]. this legal limit has not been explicitly harmonised at european level, as it was not included in directive 96/53. so far only 12-metre buses are allowed to travel freely throughout the european union. at the request of the council of ministers, the commission has drawn up a proposal to include passenger vehicles in directive 96/53, in order not only to harmonise the length of rigid buses but also to clarify further the situation as regards the removable parts in buses and trailers. this work has led to the present proposal. we must know that even in countries where buses of 15 metres in length are officially allowed, they are in practice either not allowed to enter or sent back across the border. in this sense, it can be argued that the present proposal seeks to solve the problem by establishing single market rules for the whole of the european union. obviously, there are commercial reasons for the length of the buses: a 50-foot-long [15 m] bus can carry about 67 passengers, 16 more than a 40-foot-long [12 m] bus. obviously, competition forces some carriers to take the longer buses. in cases where the bus is full, it is preferable, also for environmental reasons, to use larger buses. finally, this proposal clarifies the situation as regards the parts of buses which can be carried, in particular ski boxes, which should be included in the total length. i am personally in favour of the mandatory three axles on buses over 12 metres in length, which are likely to exceed the maximum permissible weight per axle. the three axles ensure better traffic conditions and less congestion on the roads. the other measures proposed are also satisfactory, such as the maximum length of the bus plus the trailer being equal to the length of the lorry plus the trailer. the sum of all of these is 18.75 meters at the most. it should be noted that from 1/1/2000 the maximum width of buses was increased from 2.5 to 2.55 metres. the adjustments to the maximum length of rigid buses will in all likelihood lead to the requirement for a corresponding change in legislation on rigid trucks designed for freight transport, although it is also likely that there will not be too much demand for such vehicles. the basic criteria for ensuring the manoeuvrability of buses are the concentric circle and the outswing. these criteria are laid down in directive 92/27 on the masses and dimensions of certain categories of motor vehicles and their trailers. although this directive allows for derogations by member states, it is important that these criteria are respected at european level and also apply to the longest rigid buses, as proposed in this directive. the reason is that buses, both city buses and coaches, often travel on narrow streets in city centers during rush hours, when the road is also busy with other cars, motorcycles, bicycles and pedestrians. in the event of a traffic problem for buses 15 metres long in certain places, historic centres, narrow roads, etc., the local or national authorities should mark the restrictions imposed and i think this is a good arrangement. i would like to refer to the three amendments adopted by our committee. the first provides for a transitional period during which the use of buses already approved for internal transport but which do not meet the new requirements of the directive may be authorized. if this period had ended on 31 december 2009, the period would have been extremely short, since this would have significantly reduced the running time of the already registered buses over 12 metres. this would create unacceptable economic disadvantages for undertakings which in good faith have approved or put into service vehicles before the entry into force of that directive. the second amendment considers that it should be clearly stated that these are rigid buses and not articulated buses. if the maximum length of diagonal buses is increased, it should be possible to attach detachable components such as ski boxes to buses of 12 metres. this increase in length also creates a challenge for bus manufacturers to reduce the weight of the vehicles by finding alternatives in terms of materials used and bus equipment, so that buses with a length of more than 12 metres but much less than 15 metres can also be manufactured and classified as axle-driven vehicles. there is also the third amendment, which refers to removable parts, such as ski boxes, and the ability of the bus to turn. it sets out criteria different from those of the directive because it considers that if the proposal put forward by the transport working group to amend directive 96/53 as regards the measurement methods and limit values of the gauge were applied, the maximum length of buses would be set at 14.6 metres. this is in contrast to the length of buses, at least three axles, laid down in article 1 of the directive. the buses will be 40 centimeters smaller. now, as regards the amendments tabled, i cannot agree with amendment no 4 by the liberals because, as i said to the commission, it does not conform to the spirit of the directive. the commission has already made a number of proposals to the council, and i am sure that the council will be able to accept them. the commission's proposal for a directive on the protection of workers from the risks related to exposure to asbestos is a very good one. i would like to ask the socialists and the commission to clarify my position, but the two-year transitional period seems to me to be too long. i would like to ask the commissioner whether he has not already made a statement on the subject of the european parliament's budget.
- mr president, the members of the communist party of greece will not vote in favour of mr brok's report and the other reports, because they consider the accession of the enlargement countries to be conquests, etc. but this preparation, as experienced by the people, has exactly the opposite effect. the abolition of national sovereignty and independence, the plundering of these countries by multinational capital, the addiction to unemployment, poverty, misery, corruption, prostitution and the trade in white flesh. health, education, culture, industry, agriculture are being generously of the peoples, of abolishing the social rights won with long hard struggles and sacrifices. we call on them to fight for another europe of all peoples, free of these inhuman characteristics, where
