- mr president, commissioner, today, with the reports we are discussing on enlargement, together with the brok report and today's debate on institutional reform, we can without exaggeration call this a day of celebration for europe in the 21st century. the report is a good one, but it is also a good one, and i think it is a good one, because it is a report which is very important for the future of the community. the climate of the reports is overwhelmingly positive and, in this respect, any complaining voices should be put aside. we must not look at the tree, but look at the forest. now historical time is identical with political time. now we have to make the overrun. the european parliament has been a very active participant in the process of european integration, and it has been a very active participant in the process of european integration. but beyond these general points, i would like to make a special reference to the last two mediterranean island states, cyprus and malta, which are now set to join the european union. the commission has already made a number of proposals to this effect, and i am sure that the council will be able to accept them. in this respect, the logic of enlargement will be judged not only on the basis of economic or non-economic criteria but also on the basis of respect for the more general principles which must govern our conceptions of the future of europe, of a europe of peace, of a europe of peoples, of a europe of states, of a europe of equality, of a europe of respect for human rights. in this sense, i believe that the cyprus issue is, above all, the one on which europe's ability to see its political face will be judged. the cyprus and malta issue will be the one that will prove to europe that it accepts enlargement in geographical terms, not for reasons of geographical symmetry but for reasons of political integration of a unity of peoples, a unity of cultures, a unity of perspective.

(el) the mr president, the adoption of the council directive on equal treatment in employment extends the prohibition of discrimination beyond the grounds of racial and ethnic origin to the other grounds used as a basis for discrimination referred to in article 13. the directive covers all employment, employment and selfemployment. the report by mr mann was adopted almost unanimously by the committee on employment, and this is due both to his excellent preparation and his willingness to cooperate on the necessary amendments, and for this i congratulate him sincerely. the first positive aspect of the directive is its universal approach to discrimination, which is of particular importance for the proper application of the directive. a second advantage is that it draws on the experience of non-discrimination on grounds of sex and uses the same tools and concepts as direct and indirect discrimination, effective judicial protection and the reversal of the burden of proof. it also exploits the tradition of positive action to offset disadvantages for the integration of certain people into the labour market. these guidelines of the directive, together with the fact that the 2000 directive was adopted a short while ago, demonstrate the european union's expressed desire to guarantee an open society, free from preconceived categories and, above all, from the stigma of racism, which is deeply rooted in european history. it is no accident that the action programme gives priority to understanding the phenomenon of arbitrary discrimination, thus seeking to tackle the issue at its root, which is covert racism. this is partly due to fears and partly to a long-standing tradition, which must not be ignored as one of the negative aspects of european culture and one of the main causes of conflicts, both civil and multinational. the commission has already taken a number of measures to ensure that the directive is implemented.

commission. – research shows that the creation of the single currency entails significant overall economic benefits. that is why the introduction of the euro should be seen as an investment rather than a cost for the economic operators involved. research on the potential changeover costs for individual undertakings would only provide a partial view and could be misleading. as regards compensation, the member states and the commission take the view that the public authorities and the private economic operators will each bear the costs of the changeover in their respective fields. the commission believes that all undertakings will be able to prepare themselves in time for the final changeover to the euro, including the adaptation of their computer systems and software. however, the present state of preparation is a matter for concern. indeed many smes are still not fully aware of the implications of the changeover for their businesses, particularly as regards the legal tax and accounting rules and their software. the commission and member states are currently stepping up their efforts to provide information to remedy this situation.

commission. – under the current council directive 92/20 on the deliberate release into the environment of genetically modified organisms, products containing or consisting of gmos may not be placed on the market unless consent has been given under the provisions of this particular directive. although this is not yet reflected in the relevant community seeds legislation, this principle would apply also to those seed lots of conventional plant varieties of the different genera and species, including cotton, which contain impurities of eu-unapproved genetically modified seeds. member states are responsible for inspection and control to ensure compliance with directive 90/220. the commission white paper on food safety has foreseen the need to amend the annexes of the existing directives on the marketing of seeds in order to first of all lay down the growing conditions and other requirements for purity concerning the adventitious presence of genetically modified seeds in seed lots of traditional plant varieties and secondly to lay down the details of the labelling requirements for seed of genetically modified plants. the commission services are currently preparing a draft commission directive covering these issues and including cotton seed. however, in this year's spring sowing season the commission was informed of interceptions of imported seed of conventional varieties of different species including cotton containing traces of gm seed. the member states concerned reacted differently with regard to the interceptions. as far as the measures taken by the greek authorities are concerned, i would also refer to the answer given my colleague, mrs wallström, to oral question h-742/2000. the standing committee on seeds and propagating material for agriculture, horticulture and forestry has discussed a plan for coordinated and harmonised interim action in respect of gm seed impurities in seed of conventional varieties. under this plan the member states would immediately organise testing of a significant number of seed lots of conventional varieties to determine the presence of gm seeds until the coming into force of related new community legislative measures. a set of criteria was identified to establish a common approach for sampling and testing for the presence of gm seeds and for appropriate action in the case of detection of gm seeds. the criteria are currently under discussion. this interim action plan is designed to cover the period until the coming into force of related new legislation in order to avoid an unsatisfactory situation similar to that earlier this year in the forthcoming sowing seasons.
(el) the the question we asked was clear: what action is the commission taking? the commissioner has told us some general measures that are going to be taken, etc. but there is no real, practical measure to prevent the introduction of genetically modified seeds. how do we interpret this ? of course, wishes, as we do today, good intentions, we can have. however, the absence of any practical measures to prevent imports of genetically modified material from the united states and other third countries continues. in our opinion, you should, for example, commissioner, ask that shipments be accompanied by a necessary document, a certificate from a reliable, accredited international organization or laboratory certifying that the consignments of seeds imported are free of genetically modified organisms. but you're not asking for that. here is a practical measure you could take right away.

in writing. - (el) children are the first victims of television violence. everyone now recognises the harmful impacts and consequences of the daily and ever-increasing targeting of violent tv programmes and similar multimedia products on the youngest ages, who are the most vulnerable because they have not yet developed a critical sense. the commission's text and the report make some pertinent remarks concerning the harmful effects on the psychological balance and the formation of the personality of the child and, consequently, on the risks that the following of such programmes and the consumption of such products pose for the society of tomorrow. but their proposals are limited to questions of timetable, programme classification, and they are mainly aimed at digital and subscription television by adopting the conclusions of the oxford university study on the tv programme filtering devices that parents should have in order to use them to protect their children. once again, we see this delicate and sensitive issue of protecting our children - who will be the societies of tomorrow - being commercialised for even greater profits. the full responsibility for protecting children rests solely with the parents, and there is no mention of the production and broadcasting of programmes which may be harmful to children. but how can parents, assuming they can get their hands on this filtering device, protect their children from so-called children's programmes, which contain, for the most part, scenes of violence, some of which are worse than horror films? how will they protect them from the violence that is invading the multimedia and internet sectors? the commission's communication recognises the problem but does not propose any concrete measures, because it is locked into the logic of market and competition liberalisation, and the proposals it may make do not touch the crude reality - especially for children - of the total license enjoyed by multinational companies, which, in their quest for profit, do not care at all about the impacts of their programmes and products and transfer all responsibility to parents, even inviting them to consume new products to protect their children. the only reference to companies is that they are asked to adopt a self-regulatory code. we agree with some of the interesting proposals in the angelilli report on child protection, and with the need for a detailed study of the social and legal aspects of the protection of minors.
in writing. - (el) i would like to congratulate mrs angelilli on the proposals she has put forward in the report on the establishment of control mechanisms to protect children from watching violent or other programmes which are not recommended for their age. i fully agree with them, but i would point out that they are only of minor importance when the main text is not respected, since these proposals are contained in a non-binding commission communication while the binding 'television without frontiers' directive (97/36/ec), to which they refer, is violated in many ways and by almost all member states. it is therefore imperative that all member states comply with community legislation as soon as possible. after an investigation i personally carried out in the relevant commission departments, i found that eight member states (austria, belgium, denmark, finland, portugal, sweden, spain and england) apply community legislation, while the others (france, germany, greece, ireland, italy, the netherlands and luxembourg) violate it in various ways. i also agree with our colleague that these measures should not be limited to the television sector alone but should now apply to the internet, which no longer has anything to "envy" to television, since the many applications it offers also include the projection of films. to make these proposals practical, we need to develop filtering and classification systems which will enable parents to choose the programmes which are suitable for their children. it is important to encourage industry in this respect through international cooperation and to raise awareness among parents, educators and children.

- mr. president, i'm going to talk about the islands. when the intergovernmental conference in amsterdam inserted a special reference to island regions in article 158 on economic and social cohesion, adding the word 'islands' as a special category to the regions receiving special attention in the context of cohesion policies, we here in the european parliament, who had played a leading role in this development, considered that a new era had begun for island regions. our conviction was reinforced by the adoption of declaration 30 of the new amsterdam treaty, which states that the conference recognises that island regions face different problems, due to their insular nature, which because they are permanent impede their economic and social development, and adds that the conference also recognises that community legislation must take account of these problems. unfortunately, our hopes have been dashed, partly because the islands are paying for the different translations that have been made for this particular point. according to the first version, this wording covers all islands, whereas according to the second version it covers only those which are in difficulty. it is obvious that the second version is not correct, firstly because economically backward regions are covered by the structural funds anyway and secondly because if this interpretation were valid there would be no reason to adopt declaration 30. mr president, as chairman of the committee on regional policy, i would like to remind you that the european parliament has always stressed the permanent handicap of island regions, which is their isolation. commissioner barnier, who was in crete last weekend and whom i should like to congratulate on his report, saw the ships docked in the ports of crete. they've been tied up for five days now and the goods aren't being moved. the cost of transport from an island to the mainland is three times as high as it would be if there were a road connection. farmers are often forced to grow a crop, and when the crop doesn't do well, they are destroyed. a kilo of oil, a staple of the greek islands, costs less than a pack of cigarettes. and let us not forget that these people sometimes die on the small islands when the weather conditions do not allow them to be transported to the centre. we must therefore move in that direction.

in writing. - (el) for my country, greece, the vine is of particular importance, since it is an inseparable feature of its landscape and has been inextricably linked to its civilisation and economy for centuries. at the same time, greece is the only member state producing and exporting dried grapes. the planting material for the vine must meet high standards, both from the point of view of the phytosanitary status and the certification of the variety or clone, since it is the initial planting material of the vineyard for the winegrower who wants to obtain good quality products and ensure his income. the directive on plant propagating material for vines undoubtedly needed to be amended, since, 32 years after its establishment, enormous changes had taken place both on the market and at the technical and scientific level. the changes dictated by the reality and needs of the sector had to take place, but always taking into account the interest of the producer and the consumer, which is not the approach adopted in the report. more specifically, it has not been scientifically established that genetically modified grape varieties are completely safe for consumer health and the environment, and the fact that authorisation is granted only after all appropriate risk prevention measures have been taken does not constitute a guarantee. nowhere is it specified who issues the authorisation or what checks will be carried out. as research into genetically modified organisms has developed under the control of large multinational companies, which are only interested in increasing their super profits without concern for the protection of health and the environment, fears about the seriousness and honesty of these so-called authorisations and controls are reinforced. it is proposed to allow trade in lower standard wine-growing material when temporary difficulties arise, but without specifying what these might be. or, by way of derogation, to place standard reproductive equipment on the market when the needs cannot be met by other categories. the key point in these cases is that the lower standard propagating material results in a degraded vineyard and the winegrower compromises his income while the trader does not have any losses since he sells even a degraded product. as regards the definition of 'clone', it is not sufficient, because, in fact, the clone is a subdivision of the variety with specific characteristics that must be notified, a requirement that is not covered by the given definition. we note that the committee of wine experts has given a more recent, more complete definition. as regards scientific techniques such as micromultipliation (in vitro), provision should be made to exclude the production of material by in vitro culture from meristems, since experience and tests carried out by institutes over many years have shown that mutations are observed at these points in the plant.
in writing. - (el) excessive competition has become the new obsession of neoliberalism. the principle of social regulation by the laws of the market alone tends to abolish the human being and his creativity as subjects of the economy and production, and is a fortiori disinterested in the welfare of individuals in favour of that of enterprises. it is well known that even if the rules of competition were transparent and applicable to all - which has never been the case and never will be - the conditions under which everyone has access to these rules are not equal, so that the famous law of the jungle, the law of the strongest and the domination of the interests of the monopolies prevail. in the face of the outcry caused by the consequences of this policy, state interventions, financing and other economic and fiscal incentives partially compensate for the situation. interventions, whatever they may be, do not really affect the capitalist system and often they selectively strengthen enterprises and business leaders. but the desperate effort to abolish state aid is not aimed at these 'derogations' but at increasing the profits of already powerful firms and consolidating their position in the market without the risk of new competitors. the eu's least developed regions and sectors, the poorest social strata know that without state intervention, the development gap will continue to widen at their expense. they know that without a strong, productive and efficient public sector, there is no light at the end of the tunnel. they also know that the restructuring and liquidation of the public sector is not in their favour but serves to strengthen big capital and increase its profits. the liquidation of public assets is an important element in weakening the social factor and minimizing the resources at its disposal. in other words, along with the increase in profits, a major political weapon is passed into the hands of capital to oppose the popular demands. we consider the continuing pressure and the corresponding institutional measures, such as those proposed for the register and the evaluation of 'performance' in the field of state aid, to be inadmissible. we stress the risk that, once the eu has established control over large state aid, it will target small aid by invoking competition for smes. the choices made by large capital are characterised by a strong concern about possible control of competition by the member states and not by the eu commission. the intolerable situation for workers caused by the choices of large companies and the eu is already provoking strong reactions. the struggle against 'globalisation' and the domination of the unbridled laws of the market is increasing at an accelerated rate. attempts to further reduce the protection of workers, the all-out offensive against their acquired rights, together with the initiatives taken to minimise the specific burden of the public sector and the compensatory role that state aid can play, only increase popular discontent and lead more workers to the paths of struggle and resistance.

in writing. - (el) under the pretext of resolving certain questions concerning fruit and vegetables, the commission is proposing important changes to the relevant regulations, without the report on the results of the previous regulations, which were implemented only in 1997, being final. the commission's argument that the proposed changes settle some minor issues is completely hypocritical and misleading since the proposed changes change the entire common organisation of the market in processed fruit and vegetable products; in addition, it is getting worse for small and medium-sized producers and improving to a large extent for traders and industrialists, as well as for multinational companies. under the pretext of paying the aid directly to the producer, it is proposed to abolish the minimum price for industrial tomatoes and peaches with adherent cores. this price, without offering complete or satisfactory protection to the producer, constitutes a minimum safety valve. the commission, instead of proposing to improve the value of the levy in such a way as to ensure an effective income for farmers, is proposing to abolish it and to leave traders and industrialists uncontrolled, free to exploit farmers further and to destroy their labour and production. moreover, instead of proposing a substantial increase in quotas, the commission is proposing to replace them by the system of limits so that farmers, lured by extremely low premiums because of the co-responsibility fines, produce quantities in excess of the limits for traders and manufacturers to take at will. of course, there is no certainty that the quantities in excess of the limits that farmers will produce will be sold or that they will have a guaranteed minimum price. they thus become a further weapon in the hands of traders and industrialists to blackmail farmers and set extremely low prices for all quantities, even those not exceeding limits. quotas and limits are like scylla and charybdis, and changing them means we go from bad to worse. if we combine the unacceptably low level of national limits, which decimates premiums, with the proposed reductions in withdrawal quantities - 5% for citrus, 8.5% for apples and pears and 10% for the rest of the fruit and vegetables in the volume intended for the market - it is clear that for large quantities of processed fruit and vegetable products there will be no outlet for distribution. this would have resulted in lower prices overall, putrefaction of the produce in the fields and in the cooperative's packaging facilities, thus forcing farmers to dig up what they have grown. finally, as regards the fixing of the quantity of premiums, the commission took the opportunity of the cyclical rise in international prices for processed fruit and vegetable products during the last trading period to set the premiums at low levels which will be in force for several years, i.e. until the next amendment of the regulation. the proposals on fruit and vegetables are part of the more general framework of the anti-agricultural policy in which the european union is acting, as farmers are being persecuted. this persecution is particularly cruel to the products and small and medium-sized farmers of the south. we do not need improvements to be made to destructive proposals, as the parliament's report is trying to do. the absolute rejection of these proposals and an unconditional demand for a reorientation of the agricultural policy to the benefit of farmers and the rural world are necessary.
in writing. - (el) the problem of the increase in the temperature of our planet, resulting from the accumulation in the atmosphere of gases that increase the greenhouse effect, such as carbon dioxide, is taking on catastrophic dimensions. just three years after the kyoto conference, the european environment agency estimates that if emissions rates within the european union remain the same, not only will we fail to reduce co2 emissions by 8% between 1990 and 2010, but that they will increase by 6%, while in the united states the forecasts are even worse. this problem is rooted in the sacrifice of everyone and everything, including the quality of the environment and the climate, on the altar of capitalist exploitation of resources and excessive profit. it is therefore quite normal to try to make those who created the problem pay for the solution. the commission's statement is misleading, since it does not mention any specific policies or measures to reduce greenhouse gas emissions, but merely presents, as the rapporteur rightly points out, a general chart and list of trivial policies and measures. it reiterates that energy taxation or the tax on mineral fuels is the european union's basic strategy for reducing greenhouse gases. we do not agree with this measure because, with the distorted application of the 'polluter pays' principle, the entire cost of reducing emissions will be paid directly by consumers, i.e. workers. under the considerable pressure created by the problem, one could at best envisage the imposition of an energy tax on mineral fuels, but only as a complementary measure to an integrated policy and measures to reduce co2 emissions, with the necessary condition that the revenue from this tax will only serve to relieve workers. our firm view is that reductions in carbon dioxide and other greenhouse gases will have to be achieved primarily at the cost of the real polluters (i.e. large industries) and at the expense of their excessive profits. we also agree with the rapporteur that priority should be given to renewable energy sources and to support for public transport. we also express our absolute opposition to the system of trading emission allowances between companies and governments, because it is immoral and inefficient. we cannot conceive of creating an entire market in which pollution is traded like a commodity and in which some companies buy their excess quantities from others who have exceeded their threshold, instead of reducing their own co2 emissions. of course, in a capitalist system where everything is bought and everything is sold, even that may be possible. the european parliament's committee on budgets, which is the committee responsible for the budget, has been asked to draw up a report on the budgetary procedure.
in writing. - (el) in general terms, we, the meps of the communist party of greece, agree with the framework of elements to be included in the 6th framework programme for research, because all the initial objectives, such as the constitution of committees, the development of trials, the improvement of the control of endocrine disruptors (eds) in the environment, etc., are moving in the right direction. however, we would like to draw attention to the determination of a generally acceptable definition of endocrine disruptors, in which, in our opinion, the effects of the emitted radioactivity (accidents, radioactive waste from peaceful or military activities, unimpeded and unconditional access of scientific groups responsible for industrial uses) should be included. its effect on the growth of thyroid cancer exists, as for example in the case of bombs containing depleted uranium used in yugoslavia. similarly, a more concrete framework will have to be defined, covering research, standardization of tests, cooperation between different scientific groups in order to achieve reliable results. efforts to detect substances suspected of being endocrine disruptors will also need to be ensured and strengthened, possibly in a legislative framework. awareness-raising among the public, consumers and those in occupations exposing them to potential endocrine disruptors (farmers, industrial workers and others) must be a key concern for the success of this effort. the principle of prevention enshrined in article 130p of the eu treaty (control and removal of known or suspected endocrine disruptors and development of safe alternative substances) must also be implemented.

a few. the micro-enterprise incubator contains thousands of fragile ventures that feed human capital and ideas into large enterprises. the competition they face is enormous, and if they succeed at the production level, often coming up with new, original and imaginative proposals, what can they do about distribution networks, marketing and advertising their products? i have to say that i am not in favour of this. - mr president, the tampere european council conclusions stressed the need for cooperation with the countries of origin and transit of migrants and refugees and, in this context, the high level group on asylum and migration, under the mandate of the council, drew up six plans, including the plan for albania and the surrounding region. it is clear that the main purpose of the plan is to stop migration to european union countries and to repatriate those who are living illegally in the union. the plan therefore contains mostly measures to control and prevent migration flows. cooperation with third countries can certainly help to eliminate the causes of refugee and migrant flows, but this does not mean that efforts in this direction can exempt the countries of the union and our neighbours widen, so will the migratory pressures. neither border fortifications nor police repression can block the way for those who claim a share in the in part i, which indicates the most urgent problems, e.g. first the economy and democratic institutions must be developed, infrastructure must be created in albania and then the union must sign mainly with the protection of persecuted citizens, with the continuing violations of human rights and not with how to limit and stop migration to the countries of the union. the fact is, mr president, only real possibility and hope of survival for themselves and their families. the situation worsened when the actions of political forces, generously supported by the european union, led in 1997 to the pyramid phenomenon and depleted the people's savings and, at the same time, any credibility in the structures of the albanian state and its organization. this situation resulted in the popular uprising that effectively abolished the albanian state, causing enormous security, political and economic problems for the albanian citizens. the commission has also been asked to provide information on the situation in the former yugoslavia, and to provide information on the situation in the former yugoslavia. the situation has been aggravated by the events in kosovo and the nato bombings, which have exacerbated the problems in the region. to turn a blind eye to this situation and try to build walls around your paradise to protect it from the supposedly desperate neighbors is an attitude both inhuman and short-sighted. of course, every person wants to live in his own country, if he can live in it, in safety and dignity. but on the other hand, there's no power to stop the desperate. it would be good to abandon the repressive measures and move in two directions: firstly, to help albania to revive its economy and to recover its population there, and secondly, to legitimize the presence of albanian migrants in our countries instead of treating them as savages and in conditions of black labour, to use them in activities where we need them. there are jobs, for example in greece, which if the albanian immigrants leave they will not be able to do, especially in the agricultural sector. let us therefore make use of bilateral agreements which, in a legal and clear way, define work, the length of stay, the place of stay, pay, hours, working conditions and social security, so that migrants can also be a force for the economy of our countries and help to promote friendship and cooperation between peoples, rather than to promote nationalistic and cold-war ambitions.

in writing. - (el) the charter of fundamental rights of the european union is far behind the un declaration of human rights, or the european convention on human rights, but also far behind many constitutions of the european union member states. the claim that reference is made to social rights for the first time is not valid, since such references are found in international conventions, such as the international labour conventions. for example, article 15 states that 'everyone has the right to work', but does not recognise the right to gainful employment. - article 16 which, for the first time in an international text, recognises the freedom to conduct a business; - article 28 which gives employers the possibility of deciding on a lock-out and of keeping trade unions out of negotiations with employers; - article 3 which leaves wide latitude to all kinds of genetic experimentation; the right to education is recognised in article 14, but it is simply interpreted as the right to free education, and this only for compulsory education. this is a reduction, not an extension of rights, and the reasons are clear. the authors of the charter intend to promote, in a weighty document, their policy of aggression against what the working class has won over the last 150 years by means of struggles, sacrifices and bloodshed. the possible adoption of the charter at the forthcoming nice summit, whether incorporated into the treaties or in the form of a mere proclamation, will be a negative development. there is no doubt that its ultimate objective is to serve as a preamble to a future european constitution which, in turn, will be the crowning achievement of the eu's march towards federalisation, where, now institutionalised, decisions will be taken by a few powerful people to be applied by all. the main objective is to serve the interests of the monopoly capital of the european union to the detriment of the interests of workers and, more generally, of the people. we are aware that for some countries the charter may mean some progress on certain issues. for most countries, however, as with the international arrangements, this is a regression. our goal should be to move forward, not to move toward worse. we, the members of the communist party of greece, vote against the text which is not only useless but also dangerous.

council. - the council is aware of the growing importance of the european union's overall energy dependence and reminds the honourable member of its conclusions of may 2000. in this context, the council gives the highest priority to the continuation of its work in the following areas: promotion of renewable energy sources, energy efficiency, security of energy supply and diversification of energy sources. as regards renewable energy, a proposal on the promotion of electricity from renewable sources is currently being considered by the relevant council bodies with a view to reaching agreement at the next council meeting in december, provided that the european parliament has delivered its opinion in due time. as regards energy efficiency, the commission presented an action plan at the council meeting in may. on the basis of this plan, the council should adopt, in december next, conclusions containing a list of priority actions which should enable the commission to assess the issues on which the member states want community action or, on the contrary, prefer to confine themselves to coordinated action. in addition, the commission, in its communication of 4 october 2000 on the supply of oil to the european union, indicated that it intended to present to the gothenburg european council a plan for energy saving and diversification of energy sources which should contribute to improving energy efficiency, particularly in buildings and transport. as regards the security of energy supply, the commission intends to present, in the course of november 2000, a green paper on the future and the place of the various sources of energy in the context of a better security of energy supply in the european union. this communication will cover both nuclear energy and renewable energy. the council will give it the highest priority as soon as it is transmitted. the presidency has also convened a conference on security of supply in paris on 24 november 2000.

council. - as regards the incident involving greek and turkish aircraft during the exercise destined glory, it should be noted that this incident took place in the context of a nato exercise between two members of the atlantic alliance. it is therefore up to nato to take the measures it deems necessary to ensure that such events do not happen again. this incident was not discussed in the council of the european union. in the context of the disputes between turkey and greece in the aegean sea, the council recalled the conclusions of the helsinki european council which stressed the principle of the peaceful settlement of disputes in accordance with the united nations charter and which urged the applicant countries to make every effort to settle any border disputes they might have, as well as other similar issues. if they do not, they should bring the dispute before the court of justice within a reasonable period. by the end of 2004 at the latest, the european council will take stock of the situation with regard to any remaining disputes, in particular as regards their implications for the accession process, and to promote their settlement through the international court of justice. in general, the issues relating to the settlement of the greek-turkish disputes are part of the enhanced political dialogue in the framework of the pre-accession strategy for turkey and the accession partnership which the commission has just proposed and which the french presidency of the council - i confirm this - wishes to see adopted swiftly. the council also welcomed the fact that the turkish and greek foreign ministers, at their meeting in budapest on 31 october, agreed to continue building confidence within nato and bilaterally.

in writing. -the mediterranean has always been a particularly sensitive region, marked by major political, economic and social problems. issues relating to peaceful coexistence and mutually beneficial cooperation between states and peoples acting on an equal footing and refraining from interference in their internal affairs - thus maintaining normal interstate relations - are always the main element of all cooperation between states. the mediterranean countries outside the eu are faced with particularly acute problems of economic development. there is constant tension due to regional inequalities. the eu and the member states have a very heavy responsibility in this situation. we are actually seeing an over-exploitation of natural and human resources by developed countries - mainly the eu - and their companies. the eu and its member states are also responsible for maintaining and increasing external debt. the attempt to export the eu's socioeconomic model to non-eu mediterranean countries and to integrate them into the new world order and what is commonly called the globalisation process is a colonialist mentality. it can in no way be the basis of cooperation, because it is aimed at imposing the views of the powerful on the weak and serving the interests of the big monopolies, not the peoples and workers of the countries of this region. a telling example of this attitude is the attitude of eu member states towards immigrants from these countries, as well as others; the commission has already taken a number of measures to ensure that the community's social policy is not distorted by the fact that the social partners are not involved in the decision-making process. other examples of community policy are: the various embargoes, if not military interventions, against countries that do not submit to the options of the eu and the new world order; the exclusion of some countries from euro-mediterranean cooperation and the preferential treatment accorded to others; the commission has already made a number of proposals to the council and parliament on the subject. finally, let us not forget that in this region the eu and nato bombed yugoslavia, and that the eu, with the blessing of nato, has granted turkey the title of candidate country, even though turkey occupies 40% of the cypriot territory and disputes the sovereign rights of greece. nor must we forget our responsibilities to the palestinian people, who live in miserable conditions under the terror regime of the state of israel and continue to struggle heroically for a homeland. unfortunately, the motion for a resolution does not take these questions into account. it is based on the spirit that has animated the eu up to now, that is to say, it aims to promote the penetration of european monopoly capital, obviously to enable it to continue to plunder the wealth of the mediterranean countries outside the eu. that is why the members of the european parliament from the communist party of greece will reject it.
in writing. -the new proposed five-year programme on gender equality is the most reactionary programme that has been drawn up in recent years. it aims to fully integrate women into eu policies directed at workers. the policy of equal opportunities - which is only a facade - has not addressed the higher unemployment rates of women, nor has it prevented parttime employment and poverty, nor the underrepresentation of women in the centres of power. equal opportunities in employment, social participation and other areas cannot be conceived in the context of reactionary policies which globally undermine human dignity, nor in the context of a society of unemployment, underemployment and no welfare state, a society which sets a price on human values and turns them into commodities. we are radically opposed to the way in which the terms 'equality of opportunity' and 'equality' are used, because under capitalism both can only lead to the majority of workers being treated equally in social misery, which is intensifying day by day, the equal distribution of poverty, the general increase in unemployment, part-time employment, but also the reduction of public and free state benefits in the social services sector and, in general, the oppression of families. given the eu's anti-worker policies, the objective pursued and any positive measures taken to achieve it will have only one and the same aim: to fully integrate women into the eu's policies, which have become even more reactionary to the interests of the people. this text, which is part of the implementation of the eu's more general policies, makes it clear that equality policy must promote the full integration of women into the 'new economy', i.e. it must fully adapt them, on equal terms, to parttime employment, it must challenge stable fulltime employment and reduce the non-wage labour cost. a genuine equality policy requires the introduction of a different economic and social policy, which would place equal opportunities for men and women in prosperity rather than in poverty at the heart of its concerns, for example by reducing the retirement age for both sexes (60 years for men and 55 years for women), by strengthening and upgrading the public and free social policy sector, by eliminating the role played by the private sector in the health sector and by abolishing parttime work.

commission. – first of all, directive 96/61 on integrated pollution prevention and control. which was to be transposed in the member states by 30 october 1999, has not yet been implemented in greece. the commission has therefore sent a recent opinion to the greek authorities. without action being taken from the greek side we will pursue the procedure already underway. in relation to directive 96/62 on ambient air quality assessment and management, the commission has brought a case against greece for non-transposition before the court of justice. during the procedure the greek authorities made known that the directive had now been transposed. the commission then applied to the court of justice to withdraw the case initiated against the greek authorities. a study on air quality in athens, performed under a programme between the commission services and the municipality of athens has resulted in the installation of a monitoring network to comply with the requirements of daughter legislation under directive 96/62. the study also assessed present-day air quality in athens. during the measurement campaign elevated levels of sulphur dioxide were observed in some hot-spots. existing limit values for nitrogen dioxide and lead were not exceeded. the ozone thresholds pursuant to directive 92/72 were not exceeded in the city of athens, but it should be noted that no measurements were made in mid-summer. other monitoring data show ozone thresholds frequently being exceeded outside the athens agglomeration. i am hopeful that this situation will improve with the implementation of other community initiatives such as the recent agreement on the commission's proposal for a directive on national emission ceilings. in addition, daughter directive 99/30 must be transposed in member states by 19 july 2001. it sets future limit values for sulphur dioxide, nitrogen dioxide, oxides of nitrogen, particulate matter and lead. according to the study, current concentrations of nitrogen dioxide are higher than the limit value for nitrogen dioxide to be met by 2010 and the new limit value for particulate matter to be met by 2005 is, at present, widely exceeded. the recently adopted limit value for benzene is also exceeded. this situation is expected to improve with the implementation of community legislation, for example, in the transport sector. however, the greek authorities will have to take supplementary measures at local level to ensure that the new air quality standards are respected.

- mr president, the increase in the share of electricity produced from renewable sources is in principle a positive thing. the proposed share of renewables, 22% by 2010 compared to the current 13.9%, appears to be very ambitious and one wonders whether a directive, which is market-oriented, without parallel measures such as research support, is sufficient to achieve it. the commission has already taken a number of measures to ensure that the community's financial resources are used to the full. the directive refers to support schemes, in effect, to the award of premiums to undertakings operating in this sector, and it relies on the so-called market mechanisms for the final solution. this means that the workers will, in addition to the costs, also pay the entrepreneurs' profit, which would not have happened if the energy production and distribution sector had, as the communist party of greece insists, been public and had not been forcibly privatized at the behest of big capital. for these reasons, we believe that the objective pursued is practically impossible to achieve by the directive under consideration.

in writing. - (el) the commission and the council of the european union are largely responsible for the importance of the bse issue. apart from the negative position adopted in the first phase, when the european parliament denounced the dangers in britain, and in the second phase, when the scandal was raging in britain, the measures were in fact limited to that country, without a direct programme of strict controls and restructuring of livestock farming being implemented at european level. cases have emerged in france, because france is the only country in the european union which has applied, on its own initiative, a system of extensive controls. today we are in a state of total insecurity, a state of great anxiety about the dangers that lie in wait and about which consumers have not been informed, nor have the authorities. it is urgently necessary to: - take measures at european level; - carry out strict and thorough checks on all european union herds, but also on imported products; - remove animal meal from the diet of animals, not only cattle and sheep but also pigs, poultry and fish; - take measures so that, in this thorough restructuring of livestock farming, genetically modified soya starches from america, brazil or elsewhere do not infiltrate until they are scientifically assured of their reliability, which is not the case today.

limitation of expenditure imposed by emu, lead to measures which will burden the worker with the cost of health care. the differences between the insurance systems of the various member states and the convenience, and the situation of the world merchant marine is worsening. capital's impunity is reinforced by the privatisation of classification societies, which have been transferred to the shipowners, and by the absence of any real control by the national authorities, or even by the complicity of the latter. preventive measures for ships flying the flag of eu member states need to be taken without delay to improve the existing legislation and, above all, to ensure its application. the system of classification societies must be changed from being private to being public. it is also necessary to extend the scope of inspections and effective controls by the competent public authorities of the navigability of vessels of all nationalities crossing the territorial waters of the convenience. the control of merchant ships must be carried out by a public-state service with properly trained staff and the necessary technological equipment, with the active participation of the in writing. - (el) the safety of ships, the protection of life at sea and the environment require first of all that the impunity enjoyed by shipowners be tackled, that very strict measures be taken to control the state of the ship and that severe sanctions be imposed on shipping companies that violate safety rules, that the conditions of seafarers be improved, that they be given appropriate training and that the number of seafarers on board be guaranteed to meet the increased needs of ships. the introduction of double hulls for ships can contribute to safety and environmental protection, but it is not a panacea and cannot in itself guarantee navigability. we consider that the introduction of the differentiated charging system for port and pilot fees for double-hull oil tankers should not be to the detriment of the revenues of port and other treasuries to which the current fees are paid, but should instead lead to the maintenance of the current level of fees for double-hull vessels and to an increase in the percentage of fees for single-hull vessels. unfortunately, the report's proposals do not provide for this, and they make it even more difficult to withdraw over-aged vessels. we believe that the proposal to raise the capacity ceiling for vessels which are exempted from the provisions of the commission regulation for vessels of less than the marpol ceiling from 600 to 3 000 tonnes will increase the number of vessels which pose risks to safety and environmental protection. these vessels are operated in particular in regions where high frequency maritime transport is developing. for the reasons i have just mentioned, we, the members of the communist party of greece, voted against the report.

- madam president, health as the greatest good must be preserved and restored by all means. to this end, medical science must have all the means at its disposal to ensure a high level of protection of individual and public health. unfortunately, despite the measures taken, new threats and diseases are emerging, and often existing medicines are not enough to deal with them. it is the duty of the medical community to seek modern or more effective ways to address these challenges. to this end, clinical trials are essential, provided they are conducted in a way that safeguards individual dignity and psychological integrity. today, differences in laws, regulations and administrative provisions in the member states create barriers and competition between pharmaceutical companies to ensure that new medicines are available to patients in a timely manner. this is why harmonization at european level is needed. the european parliament, recognising the importance of this directive, is trying to overcome the contradictions in order not to delay the legislative process by focusing on the following safe conditions for the conduct of clinical trials: the written consent of the subjects of the clinical trial, the consent of the guardians or legally responsible persons, and the consent of the treating physician for the participation of persons with a mental retardation or a psychiatric condition which deprives such persons of the possibility to give consent; the expected benefit of the medicinal products tested outweighs any side effects; the participation of children in clinical trials should be treated with due seriousness as it is a particularly sensitive area. finally, and here i express my dissatisfaction because this is not included in the council's compromise proposals, i believe that the principal investigator for the conduct of the clinical trial should be a doctor. because only doctors have the knowledge and experience to assess the benefit and deal with the side effects and risks during the clinical trial. of course, other relevant specialists may be involved in the clinical trial team. i would like to congratulate the rapporteur, mr liese, on his report and thank him for his attempt to reconcile the contradictions.
commission - madam president, i would like to thank and congratulate the rapporteur, mr liese, for the excellent report he has presented, and the committee on the environment, public health and consumer protection for the work they have done. the commission would be pleased if, after so many efforts by all concerned, it were possible to achieve harmonization of the requirements for the conduct of clinical trials at community level. this is particularly important in terms of protecting public health. as you know, since 1965 community pharmaceutical legislation has required that medicinal products be authorized before they can be marketed. the granting of this authorization is based on compliance with three fundamental criteria: quality, safety and efficiency. this requirement serves two main purposes: protection of public health and freedom of movement. the conduct of clinical trials, in accordance with the principles of good clinical practice, makes it possible to verify the efficacy and safety of medicinal products. good clinical practice has been internationally harmonised in the form of guidelines adopted by the international conference on harmonisation, which brings together representatives of the european union, the united states and japan. however, since these guidelines are not legally binding, they are applied in very different ways in the member states. this creates difficulties for both participants in clinical trials and for manufacturers who want to conduct such trials in the european union. in september 1997, the commission therefore proposed a legislative framework, based on article 95 of the treaty, to harmonise the procedures for clinical trials in the european union. the commission amended its proposal in april 1999 to take account of the opinion of the european parliament. the council's common position, adopted in july 2000, is essentially based on the commission's amended proposal and incorporates most of the amendments suggested by the european parliament and taken up by the commission, which fully understands the concern of the european parliament which proposes amendments which are mostly aimed at improving the protection of subjects participating in clinical trials and in particular those who cannot give their consent, namely children and adults with disabilities. since these populations are in dire need of appropriate medicines, the challenge is to be able to carry out trials while ensuring their protection. the commission has also proposed that the council should adopt a decision on the conclusion of the agreement on the conclusion of the agreement on the common fisheries policy. the commission may accept all the amendments to the compromise. they are in line with the commission's objectives, namely the protection of public health in the eyes of clinical trial participants, the need to facilitate the establishment of clinical trials within the european union, and the need for the pharmaceutical industry to be internationally competitive. in conclusion, the commission can accept compromise amendments 28 to 43. it can also accept amendments nos 1, 2, 3, 6, 7, 8, 9, 10, 12, 13, 14, 16, 19, 21, 23 and 24. the commission can also accept in principle amendments nos 5, 11, 15, 17, 25, 26 and 27, but it cannot accept amendments nos 4, 18, 20 and 22.

which will run from 2001 to 2006, is not yet ready and is not expected to be in force for some time. it was therefore reasonable to ask for the extension of previous programmes, which were of course important programmes for cancer, for aids, for diseases such as pollution diseases, or even for the monitoring of infectious diseases, and in general programmes which are of great importance for public health. it was important to expand because otherwise we would have a big gap in public health issues and programmes. it is not to be forgotten that the community acquis of these programmes will in fact be transferred to the new health programme currently being drawn up and it is therefore right that they should be extended. what was agreed with the council and the commission and, of course, with the environment committee of parliament, in order to avoid a second reading and to be able to continue these programmes without any disruption so that there is no gap, was to extend the four programmes by two years, as the commission had requested, but to give the money annually, so that if in the interim the new health programme is approved and put into effect, then of course the extension will not be necessary and the money will be used for the second year. if, of course, and i do not believe it, the new programme has not yet started, then of course we will go ahead and have the money for the second year. we have therefore agreed on these matters, we have agreed that both programmes, which expire in december 2001, will be extended for one year, and we have also agreed on the amount, which is not different, i can say that it is by far less than what the european commission had originally asked for. i would therefore like to think that we have moved very quickly and with very good cooperation. i would say that we have perhaps inaugurated a new way of working in matters of codecision. instead of waiting to reach reconciliation after a long time, we actually did reconciliation in advance. i would also suggest this to the european commission and to the council and to the committees of this parliament. it is very good to be able to prepare the ground in advance, to go as far as possible, to be very close, so that even if a second reading is needed, we are very close to the point of taking decisions. i would like to thank my colleagues in the other parties, and of course my colleagues in the committee on the environment, because we really had this remarkable convergence of views and we were able to move quickly so that there was no gap in public health. and i would like to believe that we will proceed in the same way, using this method for the new health programme, so that we can really make progress on these issues, that european integration, which we all talk about and say often, is not something abstract, it is not an integration which only happens in matters of external relations, only in matters of defence. it's a series of convergences. it is not possible to imagine a unified european union if you do not have convergence in the health sector, in the education sector and in other areas. so i would say we are going slowly. we know that health is the responsibility of the member states, but the european commission also has a great deal of responsibility under the treaties for the public health of the european citizen. so we are doing the right thing. i see these programmes as a beginning of convergence and i would say that it would be a good thing, because european citizens will indeed benefit a great deal from actions in the field of public health.

the council has adopted a common position on the revision of directive 90/220/ec. the new text provides for much stricter conditions for the use and placing on the market of genetically modified organisms. the commission believes that this new directive, once it has entered into force, will improve the present situation. the council, for its part, wants the directive to be adopted quickly in order to provide a more stringent framework for the introduction of new gmos into the european union. the council also wishes to recall that it is the commission's responsibility to monitor the application of community legislation. in implementing the directive through their national legislation, member states will have to ensure the correct application of the directive and send the commission, at the end of each year, a brief factual report on the monitoring of the use of all products placed on the market, in accordance with article 18 of the directive. the member states and the commission also meet regularly to exchange information on experience gained in preventing risks from the release of gmos into the environment. finally, at the council meeting on the internal market, consumer affairs and tourism on 30 november, the commission presented an information report on the labelling and traceability of gmos. the council will examine the commission's proposals at the environment council on 18 and 19 december.

commission. - the commission informs the honourable member that a complaint is pending concerning the thessaloniki metro project. the complainant alleges that there are breaches of the community provisions on state aid and public procurement. as the honourable member knows, the filing of a complaint does not in itself affect the progress of the project. the commission considers that questions concerning delays in the implementation of the project should be addressed to the competent greek authorities. as regards the aspects of the case from the point of view of the public procurement provisions, the commission services set out their assessment in a letter to the complainant, which will be sent in the coming days. we are therefore not in a position at the moment to provide the honourable member with the information requested. the commission is also assessing the compatibility of the project financing with the state aid rules of the treaty and has not yet taken a decision on this. the greek government does not appear to have reached agreement with the concessionaire on all the conditions for financing the project. the commission has not yet received any notification under article 88 (3) of the treaty. the commission is therefore not in a position to examine all the facts of this case and to take a final decision expressing its opinion on the situation. more generally, the commission would like to point out to the honourable member that the examination of a complaint of such complexity must be carried out with the utmost care. the commission is therefore following the case closely within the procedural framework provided for by the relevant provisions of the treaty and secondary legislation. as regards the actual financing of the project, the commission would inform the honourable member that the community has already financed the implementation of the preliminary works for the project to the tune of eur 5.8 million under the second community support framework for the erdf. the request for financing the balance is contained in the operational programme proposed by the greek authorities, entitled 'road axes, ports, urban development', which was submitted to the commission under the community support framework 2000-06. the commission is expected to adopt the proposal early in 2001, so in the near future. it provides that the thessaloniki metro project can be co-financed by the erdf for a maximum of eur 122 million, while the national public contribution will be approximately eur 122 million and the concessionaire will have to contribute eur 413 million.

the commission. before i turn to this report, i should like to thank the rapporteur, mr dary, very warmly. i would also like to thank the other members of the committee on agriculture and rural development and the other committees that have worked on this. the proposed scheme is intended to replace the 12 sectoral support schemes adopted by the council over the years. the aim is to harmonize and simplify the existing rules, which were not uniform. we are using the same approach to promotion that is already being used outside the european union. the union thus also acquires a flexible instrument of support in the internal market which can be used horizontally. this means that the old product-oriented strategy is being replaced by a more thematic approach. such promotion at european level can be a useful complement to the member states' measures by addressing, for example, the following issues: quality, nutritional aspects, food safety, labelling, traceability, protected designations of origin, protected geographical indications, organic products, integrated farming products, etc. the commission has already made a number of proposals to the council, and i am sure that the council will be able to accept them. this concept of thematic information and promotion measures, complementing national and regional measures, requires a strong commitment from the member states by co-financing and by supporting the management and control of the measures to be implemented. the commission has already made a number of proposals to this effect. firstly, amendments which the commission can accept. secondly, amendments which are superfluous because their content has already been covered by these general formulations of the proposal, which are also appropriate for a framework regulation. thirdly, a group of amendments which the commission also rejects because they do not fit into this proposal or are undesirable in view of their consequences. the commission can accept amendments nos 2, 4 and 10, which call for information on the production methods of agricultural products and foodstuffs in such campaigns. amendment no 5 calls for general guidelines which define only the essential elements of the programmes concerned. the commission is open to the possibility of adopting this idea in the wording of article 5 (3) of the proposal. amendments nos 18 and 24 first part, which reduce the time intervals for reviewing the eligible topics and products from three years to two years in order to improve the efficiency of the support instrument. these are the amendments which the commission can accept. i come now to the amendments which are already covered by the proposal and which will therefore not be adopted. the commission has not yet received any information on the number of applications for interim measures. the commission has understood very well the considerations on which these amendments are based. the commission has already made a number of proposals to the council, and i am sure that the council will be able to accept them. i should like to comment briefly on the amendments to paragraph 2 of part two and to paragraphs 11, 13 and 28. the explicit mention of regional origin or other specific indications would be too restrictive of the scope of the regulation. amendment no 25 must also be rejected. however, as regards the question of when the new system should come into force, the commission is prepared to consider transitional provisions. the last group of amendments which the commission cannot accept concerns amendments which are not in line with the proposal, which could have undesirable consequences and which propose administrative procedures which are not in line with the usual practice. the following table sets out the details of the applications: amendment no 1 is not legally correct because article 299 of the ec treaty can only be applied to derogations from other articles of the ec treaty. this case is not here. amendments nos 3, 16, 17 and 24 in the second part could lead to misunderstandings as to the objectives of our support policy. amendment no 6 and amendment no 33 cannot be accepted because, given the limited resources available, the eu's share of the co-financing would be simply too high. this would also reduce the co-responsibility of the other partners. as regards amendments nos 9 and 22, which call for the establishment of a special management committee, and amendments nos 7, 19, 23 and 27, which call for further changes in procedure, it must be said that the commission must adhere to coherent procedural rules. i am glad to see that the proposal has been widely accepted by you. the community will thus soon be able to put into practice an important new instrument of support, thanks to your help and your opinion.
- mr. president, i'm sorry to interrupt you. without further ado, it looks like i'll be the last speaker of the year 2000. i have noted in part b of the report by the committee on budgets a completely unusual error which, in my opinion, should not be allowed to stand. in the greek text mrs jutta haug appears as rapporteur, that is to say as a man, and mr markus ferber as rapporteur, that is to say as a woman. double sex change in one day and without surgery, i imagine it's never happened before in the world and maybe it should be recorded in the scientific record, unless correction is made and the change is prevented.
